| DAT-13 | **P2 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-16 (refund refuses an inconsistent ledger; partial-refund accounting stays open)** | Refunds lack a double-refund guard: the refund recomputes `paid_cents` with a floor of 0 and guards only on `status='confirmed'`, with no partial-refund accounting and no unique constraint — so receipts can be understated while staying arithmetically legal. Two payment/obligation updates also carry no `tenant_id` predicate, safe only because FORCE RLS is on. | `routes.js:1039-1051, 652, 1046` | `refunded_cents` column + CHECK, partial unique index per refund event, `AND tenant_id` as defence in depth |

| DAT-22 | **P1 — NEW 2026-09-14, found while fixing a regression my own migration 082 caused** | **The system cannot identify a student from a login, and a NAME is not an identity — yet a name is what the resolution uses.** `app.users` has no `user_id`/`student_id` column and `app.students` has none pointing back, so there is **no structural link from a student's login to their roster row**. `family.child_link` links *parents* to students; it does not link a student *user*. The door therefore resolves the signed-in user to a student **by name**, scoped to tenant and class grade. Measured on the dev database: **375 students, but only 76 distinct names** — roughly five rows per name — so a name match identifies a *name*, not a child. The seed makes this vivid: **"Diya Nair" is six rows, three of them in grade 8.** A submission (or any student-scoped write) by one section-sibling can therefore be attributed to another. It is deterministic (lowest `admission_no`), tenant-scoped and auditable, but it is **not correct identification**, and no test can make it correct because the data needed to disambiguate does not exist. | `server/src/plugins/teacher.classroom/index.js:42-88` (`resolveStudent`); `server/src/plugins/pack.dpdp/index.js` (same resolution, for the same reason); live `app.students` (375 rows / 76 names) and `app.users` (no link column) | Add `app.students.user_id` (nullable, `UNIQUE (tenant_id, user_id)`, composite FK to `app.users`) and backfill it at provisioning, so a student login resolves **structurally**. Until then every name-based resolution must stay explicitly labelled as an approximation in its own code, and **new student-scoped writes must not be built on a name match** — the erasure path already avoids this by matching on `guardian_phone`, which measured **375 distinct values across 375 students** (no duplicates at all). The asymmetry is the point: a phone identified the subject exactly, and I nearly extended the name-based pattern to a second door because it was already there. |

### 5.5 API contract & integration surface — **54/100**

Runtime discipline here is well above ad-hoc: `FOR UPDATE` in 48 places with correct lock ordering (obligation before the one-open-payment check; gateway-leg-first so a refused refund never touches the ledger), HMAC webhooks with DB-level replay dedupe, the library last-copy race closed by locking the book row, reset-token redemption made race-safe by a guarded `UPDATE … WHERE consumed_at IS NULL RETURNING id`, and honest 503s on DB outage. But the *contract* surface is essentially absent — an external integrator would have to read 12k lines of JS to learn the shapes, and could not write a retry, a page loop, or branch on an error type.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| API-1 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-13 (§8)** | **The tenant-wide event stream is readable by `student` and `parent`.** `GET /events/lineage` is gated on bare `me` with no role filter, returning `label`, `emitted_by`, `subject` and `correlation_id` — and those labels embed other families' data (payment and receipt amounts, `PTM booked — <child>`, `Book issued → <student>`, leave requests, `safety.report.filed.v1`). The sibling feed `/events` correctly requires ten staff roles, so this is an inconsistency rather than a policy. Verified first-hand at both doors. | `server/src/routes.js:1394` vs `:1332`; label sources at `routes.js:631, 655`; `teacher.support/index.js:295, 722`; `library/index.js:156` | Mirror the `/events` role set, plus a per-actor filter for non-oversight roles |
| API-2 | P1 | **No machine-readable contract anywhere, and `zod` is a phantom dependency.** 268 route registrations, **zero** with a Fastify `schema`; no OpenAPI/Swagger; no versioning; `zod` is declared in `package.json` but imported nowhere (verified). No request validation, no response contract, no generated client, and no way to know a field is optional or that `billed_cents` arrives as a string. | 268 registrations across `app.js`/`routes.js`/plugins; `server/package.json`; grep `from 'zod'` → 0 | `@fastify/swagger` + schemas on the read plane first; delete or actually use `zod` |
| API-3 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-15 (atomic `UPDATE … RETURNING`; `tests/mfa-recovery-race.test.js` races two requests, mutation-probed to 2-successes under the old shape)** | **MFA recovery codes are not actually single-use.** The `UPDATE … SET consumed_at` **discards its result** and `usedRecovery = true` is set unconditionally, so two concurrent requests with the same code both issue a session. The 8-code enable loop is also untransacted with each insert unchecked, yet codes are returned to the user regardless of how many landed. | `server/src/app.js:668-681` | `UPDATE … RETURNING id`, branch on `rowCount === 1`; wrap enable+inserts in one transaction |
| API-4 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-15 (`requestTimeout`/`connectionTimeout` + `AbortSignal.timeout(8000)` on both Google fetches; asserted in `tests/mfa-recovery-race.test.js`)** | **No HTTP request timeout, against a single 20-connection pool.** The Fastify instance sets only `logger` and `trustProxy` — no `requestTimeout`, `connectionTimeout` or socket cap (verified) — and `app.js:380` fetches Google's userinfo endpoint with **no `AbortSignal`**, unlike every other outbound call in the tree. A hung response pins a handler; enough of them exhaust the one shared pool and take down the entire API, webhook receivers included. | `server/src/app.js:24, 380`; `server/src/db.js:7` (`max: 20`) | `requestTimeout: 30_000`, `connectionTimeout: 10_000`, `AbortSignal.timeout(8000)` on that fetch |
| API-5 | P1 | The "machine identity" surface reaches exactly **two** doors (`GET /me`, `GET /dataset`); every other door 403s and all non-GETs are refused. There is no programmatic read path to students, attendance, fees or audit, so any customer integration is forced onto a human JWT with a 12h TTL and a browser CSRF cookie. | `server/src/app.js:81-96` | Extend the scope table with a documented read plane; publish the scope vocabulary |
| API-6 | **P2 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-16 (`setNotFoundHandler` + stable `code`/`requestId`; the 17 `bad()` copies remain)** | **No contract on errors:** prose-only bodies, no `code`, a `bad()` helper **re-defined 17 times** across 17 files in three signature variants with 444 call sites — the only machine-readable `code` in the whole surface is 2 sites. Plus ≥6 distinct response shapes and Fastify's default for unmatched paths (no `setNotFoundHandler`), and the catch-all drops `err.code`. A client cannot distinguish "retry me" from "fix your request" without string-matching English prose the codebase deliberately rewrites for tone. | grep `bad(` → 17 definitions, 444 sites; `server/src/app.js:899-900, 1010-1014` | One shared `bad()`, a frozen `ERROR_CODES` enum, a not-found handler |
| API-7 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | Shared `pageParams()` now converts invalid, fractional, negative and oversized limit/offset values to safe bounded defaults before they reach PostgreSQL. | `server/src/routes.js:pageParams` | Extend the helper to remaining paginated doors |
| API-8 | **P2 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-16 (shared `pageParams` + `hasMore`/`OFFSET` on the read doors; the rest stay hard-capped)** | **No pagination standard.** Only 3 of 268 doors accept a page parameter; everything else is a hard-coded `LIMIT` (100/200/500) with no way to page and no `total`, `hasMore` or cursor — including sensitive doors (`/communications`, `/cases`, `/fees/obligations`). | `server/src/routes.js:601, 794, 1344, 1358, 1374` | A documented envelope + shared parser with a hard max |
| API-9 | **P2 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-16 (`Idempotency-Key` on payment initiation and case creation; other mutations stay implicit)** | **No idempotency mechanism on any mutation door** (grep → 0 hits). Retry safety is implicit and per-door: confirm and void are idempotent and attendance uses `ON CONFLICT`, but a retried payment creation, a double-submitted case, or a doubled library loan produces a second row with no client-side dedupe. | `server/src/routes.js:643, 567, 402, 811` | Honour `Idempotency-Key` with `(tenant_id, key, request_hash, response)` |
| API-10 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-16 (300-row register cap; `tests/next10-behavior.test.js`)** | Unbounded arrays on mutation doors: `/attendance/mark` validates only `Array.isArray` with **no cap**, so one caller writes and confirms 5,000 students in a single transaction (a `Promise.all` of 5,000 upserts on one client, plus a per-absent-student insert loop), and a `null` element throws → 500. Sibling doors do cap (300 rows). | `server/src/routes.js:380-418` vs `:379` | A shared `MAX_BATCH`/`MAX_JSON_BYTES` and a boundary assertion helper |
| API-11 | P2 | **Auth state changes are not atomic with their audit rows** — password change and MFA disable commit the mutation, then write the ledger in a *second* transaction, so a crash between them leaves a changed credential or a dismantled MFA factor with no ledger row (and `mfa_enrolled_at` set while the secret is gone). The project's own map asserts the opposite invariant. | `server/src/app.js:498-500, 628-632, 678-684` vs `docs/BACKEND-MAP.md:114` | Move the mutation inside the `withUser` callback |
| API-12 | P2 | The webhook receivers share the **global per-IP limiter** with no override, and every tenant's gateway traffic arrives from the same IPs — so one busy tenant can rate-limit another tenant's payment events with a 429 the gateway reads as retryable, with no local queue or DLQ. | `payments.upi/index.js:63`; `payments.razorpay/index.js:144`; `server/src/app.js:28-35` | Dedicated generous limits on both receivers; persist rejected bodies for replay |
| API-13 | P2 | Dunning uses check-then-insert against a unique index with no `ON CONFLICT`: two concurrent runs both pass the guard, the loser throws `23505` inside `withUser`, and the **entire batch** rolls back and 500s — while the audit row claims a dedup guarantee the code cannot make. The same door writes up to 200 reminders in a sequential loop. | `server/src/routes.js:1107-1135` | `ON CONFLICT (dedup_key) DO NOTHING` + one set-based `INSERT … SELECT` |
| API-14 | P2 | The pickup-OTP attempt cap setting is **dead code** — a per-tenant `pickup_otp_max_attempts` resolver exists with no call site, so a school setting `1` still gets 5. The kernel OTP door has the same check-then-increment race across two connection paths. | `plugins/ops.visitors/index.js:25, 180-184`; `server/src/app.js:342-345` | Use the resolved setting at the guard; make the counter one guarded `UPDATE … WHERE attempts < $2 RETURNING` |
| API-15 | P3 | Whole-API JSON parsing is a side effect of two optional payments plugins registering a content-type parser; `tx()` (an RLS-bypassing owner-role helper) is exported with **zero** call sites; the BI cache is per-process, so with >1 replica the same request returns different numbers than the manifest's single staleness bound promises; `/notifications/read` returns `{ok:true}` without checking `rowCount`. | `plugins/_shared/raw-body-parser.js:17`; `server/src/db.js:22-35`; `plugins/ops.bi/index.js:15-25`; `server/src/routes.js:1349-1354` | Install the parser in `buildApp()`; delete `tx()`; document or move the cache; 404 when nothing changed |

### 5.6 Product completeness — **47/100** *(can a school run a full term on this?)*

> **Provenance note.** This area was first scored 60 by the lead auditor. A dedicated product-completeness lane then re-audited it independently against the live database and scored **47**; that lower figure is adopted here because it is better evidenced, and every row it added has been re-checked by the lead auditor before inclusion. The lane's full report is at `.audit/product-last10-report.md`. The overlap between two independently-run audits was near-total, which is the strongest available evidence that this is not one auditor's opinion.

This is the highest-weighted area because it is the one the customer experiences, and it splits sharply. **Breadth is genuinely strong** and would be respectable in a funded product: admissions funnel with conversion that creates student + guardian + enrollment history with provenance, fee plans with fan-out and regeneration, obligations, payment initiation, confirmation, refunds with gateway legs, reconciliation sweeps and a dunning queue; attendance; exams with CBSE board policy, moderation, freeze and publications; timetable with clash detection and substitution; transport GPS with route/stop lineage; visitors with a pickup-OTP vault; clinic; HR leave; PTM booking; safeguarding with an anonymous safety box; DPDP consent ledger, SAR workflow and erasure journal; UDISE statutory export with a `not_recorded` honesty flag; Tally daybook export; and BI read models. Onboarding is one atomic transaction. That is real, and it is why this is not a rescue job.

**Depth fails at exactly the points a school would notice in week one.**

| ID | Sev | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| PRD-1 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-14 (verified claim-by-claim; pinned by tests/comms-honesty.test.js 7/7, plus PRD-13's dispatcher work and OPS-5/B2's channel gate)** | **The platform cannot actually communicate with parents.** Every outbound channel used to default to a provider that sends nothing while reporting success, so OTP login, absence notices, parent notices and dunning reminders — the product's core promise — were success-shaped no-ops out of the box. | `rails.sms/index.js:9-14, 81-90`; `rails.email/index.js:145, 152`; `app.js:296-310, 552-563` | CLOSED (verified 2026-09-14): each of the three claims in this row was re-read in the code and pinned by a test that fails on regression — the door's channel gate and constant key set (OPS-5/B2), the dispatcher's sandbox-is-not-a-delivery rule with its anti-overreach tests (`tests/comms-honesty.test.js`, 7/7), and the sandbox gauge (`schoolos_comms_sandbox_sent`). The row's own root cause for SEC-0 (a sandbox send keeping the full body, persisting live reset tokens verbatim) is closed by SEC-0's fix. **Nothing in this row required new work — which is itself the finding: it stayed open because a P0 that is genuinely fixed by a later round's work reads exactly like one that was never started, and only re-reading the code distinguishes them.** |
| PRD-2 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-14 (migration 083 + `routes.js` + tests/prd2-class-assignments.test.js, 10/10)** | **Teachers could not mark attendance, because there was no class-assignment model.** `routes.js:442-451` refused every non-principal role and said why in as many words: the only assignment-shaped surfaces that existed (`app.timetable_slots`, `app.timetable_substitutions`) carry `teacher_name` but **no grade/section binding at all**, so no query could answer "which classes does this teacher own". The code was right — which is why this needed a schema change, not a permission edit. Flipping the role check without the table would have replaced a 403 with an unaudited cross-class write. | `routes.js:442-451` (§3 is the same root cause); live `pg_constraint` | CLOSED — migration **083** creates `app.class_assignments` (tenant_id, teacher_id, grade, section) with a composite tenant-aligned FK to `app.users(tenant_id, id)` (relies on 081's `users_tenant_id_id_key`), a partial unique index so **one class has one owner**, and a deterministic seed so the register is usable on a fresh database without an empty-table dead end. The **form-teacher model** was chosen over per-subject because daily attendance is taken once by the person who owns the class, and the timetable has no class column to key per-subject rows from anyway. A NAME-BASED SHORTCUT WAS REJECTED: `timetable_slots.teacher_name` is free text ('D. Rao', 'Priya Iyer') that happens to match `users.name` today, and ownership keyed on a string is a rename away from silently reverting every teacher to the principal's office. Assignment is an administrative act with its own audited doors (`GET`/`POST /class-assignments`, `DELETE` retires rather than deletes so the history of who held a register survives). The classroom check is **per-student and derived from the student rows, never from the request payload's opinion** — a client claiming "this is 8A" while sending another class's ids is refused, and a mixed submission is refused WHOLE rather than partially written, because accepting the owned rows and dropping the rest produces a register that looks marked and is not. Verified end-to-end on the real dev database (own class 200, another class 403 naming both classes), and the guard is mutation-probed: removing the ownership check fails 5 of the 10 tests. |
| PRD-3 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-13 (§8): a separate librarian account exists, signs in, sees the module and its controls, and cannot reach finance — verified against the live service** | **The librarian account does not exist and cannot be made to work** — the exact thing asked for. There is no `librarian` role in `ROLES`; the library plugin's `STAFF` set is `['owner','principal','coordinator','facilities']`, so a **facilities** account can issue/return books and read guardians' phone numbers while the principal is the de-facto librarian; and the shipped "Librarian" custom-role preset fails at both layers (§5.2 RBAC-3). The plugin is `DEFAULT_ON` at tenant birth, so this is live before an admin decides anything. | `auth.js:60`; `plugins/library/index.js:31, 131, 174, 244-260`; `RolesAdmin.tsx:64-83`; `LibraryLive.tsx:26`; `plugin-catalog.js:44` |

Verified live end to end, plus the review findings B2/B6/B7/S1 below. Five unscheduled workers remain OPS-8. | **Password reset is permanently 503 for every tenant, and five shipped background workers never run.** The three rails are absent from the plugin catalog, so they cannot be configured at all and reset is gated on a row that is never created; separately, the day-worker, webhook-worker, workflow-worker, dunning-reminders and reconcile-payments workers appear in no compose service or cron, so outbound webhooks, workflow rules, fee dunning and payment reconciliation silently never execute in a real deployment. | PLG-3; OPS-8 |
| PRD-5 | P1 | **The product cannot be sold.** `entitlement` is stored and never checked, so any tenant can enable any plugin including paid packs; there is no plan, no pricing surface, no 402 path. The `/plugins` screen is documented as the sales surface and enforces nothing. | PLG-2 |
| PRD-6 | P1 | **Nothing can be integrated with.** API keys reach exactly two read doors (`/me`, `/dataset`); every non-GET is refused. A school's accountant, an ERP, or a parent app has no programmatic path to students, attendance, fees or audit — only a 12h human JWT. | API-5 |
| PRD-7 | P1 | School dates can be off by one day: two live attendance doors derive the date from UTC while Postgres runs `Asia/Kolkata`, so between 00:00 and 05:30 IST a client omitting `date` writes and reads *yesterday's* register and the unique conflict target absorbs it silently. | DAT-7 |
| PRD-8 | P1 | **Erasure does not erase the obvious thing.** A DPDP erasure request leaves `app.students.guardian_phone` and `guardian_name` — the most visible PII in the system — in the clear and still joined to the child, and the audit-redaction mechanism the SAR bundle promises (`audit_redact`) does not exist. No field-level encryption anywhere. | DAT-8 |
| PRD-9 | P2 | Money lacks maker-checker: one `finance` role records payments, refunds any confirmed payment, reconciles and sets fee-plan policy, with no amount ceiling and no second approver; refunds have no partial model and no double-refund constraint. | RBAC-5; DAT-13 |
| PRD-10 | P2 | Missing lifecycle for operators and tenants: no uninstall, no suspend kill-switch for a compromised publisher, 14 declared lifecycle states of which 3 are reachable, no upgrade/rollback, and no tenant offboarding path (deleting a tenant fails on NO ACTION FKs). | PLG-9; DAT-12 |
| PRD-12 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-13 (§8), whole class** | **Five live routes hard-crash a real tenant — and three are that role's own sidebar landing link.** Each dereferences a demo fixture with no `DEMO_MODE` branch: `/parent` (`ParentHome.tsx:24,131` — `seed.DEMO_STUDENT.name.split`), `/parent/chat` (`ParentChat.tsx:91`), `/student` (`StudentHome.tsx:69`), `/insights` (`Insights.tsx:171`), `/reception` (`Reception.tsx:195`). The root `ErrorBoundary` sits **outside** the router, so the whole application — sidebar included — is replaced by an error page. A parent, a student and a receptionist each lose the entire product on first login. | `.audit/product-last10-report.md` §demo-vs-live; verified by the lead auditor in the listed files |
| PRD-13 | **P0 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-13 (carrier honesty + pickup-OTP delivery closed; broadcast fan-out still open)** | **The bell never rings: fabricated delivery beyond the rail defaults.** Pickup OTP is minted, hashed, single-use — and never sent, while the audit row asserts "OTP issued to primary guardian" (release then rides an override). The `comms-dispatcher` resolves only **the first** recipient, so a 300-guardian notice reaches one and still reads `sent`. `day-worker.js:27-30` dynamically imports `money.invoicing/dunning.js`, a directory that **does not exist** — dunning is silently skipped and logged as `skipped`. `parent.assistant/index.js:33` defines `BANNED` and **never references it**, so "banned words pinned" is enforced only inside its own test file. | `ops.visitors/index.js:155,160`; `comms-dispatcher.js:64-76,129-143`; `day-worker.js:27-30`; `parent.assistant/index.js:33` |
| PRD-17 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-13 (§8); found while closing PRD-13, same defect family** | **The approvals inbox records a parent notice as DELIVERED — and announces it as SENT — without sending anything.** The action executor for `kind === 'message'` inserts the comms row directly with `status='delivered'`, `sent_at=now()`, `delivered_at=now()`, then emits an event whose label reads *"Parent notice SENT — guardians (…, approved by …)"* and returns `execution = { type:'communication', status:'delivered' }` (`routes.js:1076-1081`). Nothing is sent: there is no rail call and no queue entry — and there never can be one, because the dispatcher only claims `status='queued'`, so a row inserted as `delivered` is unreachable for delivery for the rest of its life. The audience is `[p.audience ?? 'guardians']`, a **role word, not a contactable recipient**, which is also why the row could not be delivered even if something had tried. `delivered_at` is the strongest claim the schema makes — the dispatcher itself only ever sets `sent_at` — so the principal approves a fee-circular and the timeline, the notices list and the action record all confirm it reached every guardian. | `server/src/routes.js:1076-1081` (INSERT … `'delivered'`, `now(), now()`); `scripts/comms-dispatcher.js` claims `status='queued'` only; `comms.whatsapp/index.js:44` renders `delivered_at` to users | (1) Insert as `queued` with a RESOLVED recipient list (guardian phones normalised to E.164 via `src/plugins/_shared/phone.js`) and let the dispatcher deliver it, so one definition of "sent" exists. (2) If the audience cannot be resolved to at least one real recipient, do NOT claim delivery: fail the execution visibly so the approver sees that the notice did not go out. (3) The event must say "approved and queued", never "SENT", until a carrier has it. (4) Regression: approving a message action must leave `status='queued'` with `sent_at`/`delivered_at` NULL and >=1 E.164 recipient, and one dispatcher pass must move it to `sent` with `delivered_at` still NULL under a sandbox carrier. |

| PRD-14 | P1 | **Most of the kernel is unreachable, and what is reachable is often not the database.** 157 of 271 doors (**58%**) have no frontend caller; ~23 screens read a mock brain in `services/index.ts`; 50% of live-routed screens (42/84) have no tenant data plane and 23 have no `DEMO_MODE` branch at all. The parity gate that should have caught this is **report-only in CI** (`ci.yml:56-57`) and cannot see `onboarding.js`/`settings-contract.js` (9 doors) or method mismatches. | `.audit/product-last10-report.md` §contract diff; `parity-gate.js:17-21` |
| PRD-15 | P1 | **Confident fabricated numbers are rendered to real users.** The `/ask` brain labels its canned output `verification:'Numbers recomputed from records'` (`services/index.ts:126-166`); ItAdmin renders a hardcoded health panel including "WhatsApp BSP · 99.2% delivery" under "Live service posture" (`ItAdmin.tsx:46-53`); `StudentLearning.tsx:17-41`/`StudentPortfolio.tsx:16-59` show a **real child** literal skills, marks and a teacher's quote from **no data source at all**; `Fees.tsx:47` draws a constant `TREND` chart under a "Live ledger" badge; `Migration.tsx:410` narrates another school's cutover. Fees are computed but **never charged** (library fines create no obligation), and `parent.report` selects columns that do not exist so parents see a permanently empty published-results list. | listed sites; `family.view/index.js:152-157` |
| PRD-16 | P2 | Transport is the least complete line at **~30%**: no route/stop write doors, GPS ingest door has 0 callers, the UI shows a `driver` field with no column, and `transport.gps/index.js:90-101` synthesises sine-wave positions for a quiet board that then **raise a real approval action** at the delay threshold. `payments.upi/index.js:51` builds `upi://pay?pa=schoolos@<StudentName>`, a VPA that cannot exist — a parent taps pay and the money cannot reach the school. | `.audit/product-last10-report.md` §product-line table |

**Verdict for this area:** the *workflows* are largely present and several are genuinely well built; what is missing is the connective tissue that makes them usable by the right person through a channel that actually delivers. A school could run admissions, fees and exams on this today. It could not reliably run attendance, parent communication, or a library desk — which is precisely the complaint that prompted this review.

### 5.7 Tests, gates & verification — **64/100**

This is a genuinely strong *backend integration* suite: 56 files, 392 declared tests, 2,213 assertion sites, 231 of 263 doors exercised through `app.inject` against a real Postgres with 567 raw-SQL ground-truth checks. The security, RLS, money and privacy files are better than most production SaaS suites — `l2-dpdp-erasure`, `l2-rails-live`, `exam-depth`, `teacher-copilot` and `students-lifecycle` prove real behaviour rather than echoing responses, and several assert *counts before and after* (the only reliable way to prove a side effect). The score reflects that a regression is still not caught before merge, and that the headline "green" claims cannot be reproduced.

**How much of the backend is actually verified:** by surface, 87.8% of doors (231/263) are referenced by a test; but weighting *effect* assertions over status-only ones, the untested non-door half (schema runner 0%, outbox relay 0%, backup/restore 0%, plugin contract ~15%), and a prudence haircut for the vacuous and conditional assertions below, **≈60% of the backend is verified by a test that would actually catch a regression** (defensible range 55–68%).

| ID | Sev | Finding | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TST-1 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-13 (§8): now importable, subscriber injectable, 8 tests incl. a verified regression probe** | **The event spine's core component has zero automated verification and cannot even be imported.** `scripts/outbox-worker.js` exports nothing and starts its poll loop at module top level, so importing it in a test hangs; no test spawns it. What made this durable is that the fix is a **gate, not N patches**: `tests/test-hygiene.test.js` detects the shapes (`silent-pass/bare-return-before-assert`, `silent-pass/skip-before-assert`, the tautologies) and carries an ALLOWLIST that requires a written reason per entry — so a skip that is genuinely unavoidable must say why, and a bare `return` cannot come back quietly. **The gate itself had a hole, found by walking into it:** its `tautology/trivial-truth` rule required a closing paren immediately after `true`, so it caught `assert.ok(true)` but NOT `assert.ok(true, 'reassuring message')` — which is the worse form, because the message is what a reader trusts instead of the condition. ### 5.8 Frontend↔backend coherence & honesty — **42/100**

*(Lead auditor's assessment, grounded in the frontend lane's cited evidence — see Appendix B, limit 5.)*

The core problem is a single sentence: **the client invents its own permission model because the server never tells it what a user may do.** `/capabilities` reports what is *configured* (rails present, plugins enabled), not what the caller is *allowed* (A12), so roles are re-derived in the browser — in 12 nav branches, a 43-entry prefix list, ~17 per-screen `Denied` states, and at least four different definitions of "front desk". The consequence is that every server-side role change is a frontend release, and the two layers drift silently.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-1 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-15 (deleted, not computed; a source gate in `frontend-grants.test.js` fails if the fabricated strings reappear)** | **Fabricated infrastructure telemetry is shown to customers as measured fact.** The IT-admin screen displays "14ms query p95 · Replication lag 0.2s · 3 read replicas", "Uptime · 30d 99.97%" and the claim that it is "checked every 30s from the status worker" — none of which exist: there are no read replicas, no uptime measurement, and (per OPS-3) no status worker or alerting at all. | `app/src/screens/ItAdmin.tsx:45-47, 104-114` | Delete it or compute it. This is the most damaging honesty defect in the UI because it is exactly the screen a technical buyer would trust. |
| FE-2 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | Navigation, command-palette destinations, and all authenticated live frontend routes now consult the server's effective-access answer. Concrete deep links resolve against parameterized catalog entries; unknown/demo/public routes are not treated as live staff surfaces. | `app/src/components/Shell.tsx`; `app/src/App.tsx`; `app/src/lib/effective.ts`; `server/src/effective-access.js` | Keep route-catalog parity coverage in CI |
| FE-3 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | The IT admin console now reads the protected `/metrics` endpoint and displays real process uptime, request/error totals, pool waiters, and tenant-scoped queue/event gauges. Unsupported p95/auth-failure/platform-wide migration metrics remain unavailable instead of fabricated. | `app/src/screens/ItAdmin.tsx`; `server/src/app.js:/metrics` |
| FE-10 | P1 *(renumbered from a duplicate FE-3, 2026-09-14)* | **Safeguarding contradicts itself in one screen, two lines apart**: the wellbeing queue's allowlist admits `teacher` while its own refusal copy promises the queue is shown "never to subject teachers", and the role it was built for (`counselor`) does not exist. | `app/src/screens/student/WellbeingQueue.tsx:34-36, 157`; `server/src/auth.js:60` | Add `counselor`; narrow the list |
| FE-4 | P1 | **The client cannot express what the server enforces.** A tenant's custom roles are authorized server-side but invisible and unusable in the UI: the shipped librarian preset's controls are gated on a hardcoded four-role list that excludes its own base role (A20). | `app/src/screens/LibraryLive.tsx:26`; `RolesAdmin.tsx:64-83` | Drive screen controls from the capability payload |
| FE-5 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | Settings now derives the demo-account count, identifies the names as fictional demo people, points to the shipped custom-role console, and labels the non-editor Manage action honestly. | `app/src/screens/Settings.tsx` | Keep customer-facing copy tied to live capability state |
| FE-6 | P2 | **Four divergent definitions of "front desk"** (`Reception.tsx` swaps `itadmin` out and `facilities` in; `LibraryLive` reuses that list; `PrintQueue`/`Noticeboard` add `teacher`; `VisitorsBoard` has its own), plus nav/gate drift in both directions (`/group` is owner-only in nav but admits owner/principal/finance by URL; 7 routed screens appear in no nav at all). | `Reception.tsx:65`; `VisitorsBoard.tsx:34`; `LibraryLive.tsx:26`; `PrintQueue.tsx:33`; `Noticeboard.tsx:32`; `Shell.tsx:142` vs `store.tsx:255`; `App.tsx:403-436` | One capability payload; delete the orphans |

Campus scoping (`'all'` vs `'north'`) is a client-side `ROLES`→campus map with **zero** server enforcement — an advertised scope that does not exist, the same defect class as the dead `schoolos.role` GUC. An orphaned second teacher nav also survives. | `App.tsx:504`; `Shell.tsx:620`; `state/store.tsx:186-190, 245`; `components/teacher/TeacherNav.tsx:83-99` | One notion of "my role"; implement campus scope server-side or drop the claim |
| FE-8 | P2 | **The session JWT is stored in `localStorage`** and read at boot, so any XSS is a full account takeover with no httpOnly mitigation. | `app/src/services/liveApi.ts:19, 33, 292` | Move to the cookie path the kernel already supports |
| FE-9 | P2 | **44,029 LOC with zero tests**, and no lint gate in CI (the web package defines `lint` green / `lint:eslint` red and neither runs; `tsconfig.app.json` has no `strict`, which is why six past crash families were unchecked-deref bugs). | `app/package.json:7-8`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:66-74`; `app/tsconfig.app.json`; TST-6 | vitest + route smoke suite; wire lint; enable `strict` |

**What the frontend gets genuinely right, and should generalise:** `screens/Audit.tsx:1-9` is the correct pattern — it loads from the real door, and when the door is unreachable or the role is not allowed it says so and falls back to *this session's* entries **with a visible label, never presented as the school ledger**. `coord/shared.tsx:14` is the one client gate that mirrors its server twin exactly. `plugins/Slot.tsx` genuinely enforces the 2-widget ration. The fabricated AI telemetry row was already removed from `Ai.tsx`. These four are the model for the migration in item 14 of §6.2 — the goal is not merely "move role lists to an endpoint" but "make every screen resolve through the endpoint and degrade the way `Audit.tsx` does."

### 5.9 Operations, observability & DR — **44/100**



| ID | Sev | Finding | Evidence | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPS-1 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-14 (structural: a `db-init` one-shot; asserted by `tests/ops-deploy.test.js`, mutation-verified)** `api.command` was `bootstrap.js && migrate.js && src/index.js`; bootstrap's first schema-dependent statement is `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA app` and schema `app` is created by the migration, so on an empty volume the chain short-circuited and `restart: unless-stopped` crash-looped. Schema setup now lives in its own `db-init` service (`migrate.js && bootstrap.js`), `api` is `node src/index.js` with `depends_on: db-init: service_completed_successfully`, and every worker waits on `db-init` too — a healthy database is not a migrated one. Re-inverting the order or re-pointing a worker at `api` now fails the suite (both verified). Residual: no container actually boots in CI (OPS-14). | **The only documented production start path cannot boot a fresh database.** Compose runs `bootstrap.js && migrate.js && src/index.js`, but bootstrap grants on schema `app` before migrations create it — so on an empty volume bootstrap fails, `&&` short-circuits, migrate never runs, and `restart: unless-stopped` crash-loops forever. This explicitly inverts the project's own runbook ("always migrate → bootstrap"). Verified first-hand. | `server/docker-compose.yml:45`; `server/scripts/bootstrap.js:24-26`; `server/README.md:86` | Reorder to `migrate && bootstrap`; make bootstrap skip absent relations so it is replay-safe in any order |

''`, the compose `api` service passes neither, and the db service creates no `postgres` role — a shape the project's own CI comment records. | `server/scripts/bootstrap.js:11-13`; `server/docker-compose.yml:7,25-37`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:22-25` | Pass `ADMIN_DATABASE_URL`, or add a dedicated db-init service |

Neither the rules nor the probe fabricate: a rule naming an unimplemented check is an error, and a missing column is a reported SKIP. Live on the dev DB it fires four real rules (stuck outbox ×2, undiscovered ×2, unscanned ×2, 3 migrations unapplied). | OPS-9 | **P1 — PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-16 (request-duration histogram + build id; `pg_stat_statements`/tracing stay open)** | No latency histogram or slow-query visibility exists, so the documented "p95 < 400ms" SLO is unmeasurable; no traces, no error tracking (`errors_total` only counts the error handler, so self-sent 5xx never register); `pg_stat_statements` is available but not installed. | `server/src/app.js:414-427, 966-990, 1012`; live `pg_settings` | Per-route duration histogram; enable `pg_stat_statements` + slow-query logging; add error tracking with tenant/route tags |
| OPS-10 | P1 | **One broken plugin takes down every tenant**: the loader aggregates failures and aborts `buildApp()`, and the container restarts forever — the maximum-blast-radius failure mode. | `server/src/plugins/loader.js:47-55`; `server/src/index.js:15-17` | Fail loud in CI, quarantine at runtime |
| OPS-11 | P1 — **CLOSED 2026-09-14** (the certificate reissue in the fix column is separately tracked) | No versioned build/commit identifier anywhere, so after a bad deploy you cannot answer "which build is live?"; the newest release certificate is 65 migrations stale and certifies a tree that no longer exists. | repo-wide grep for `GIT_SHA`/`BUILD_ID`; `docs/ops/RELEASE-CERT-002.md:8` | `ARG GIT_SHA` → `/health` + boot log; re-issue the certificate **· CLOSED 2026-09-14:** new `src/build-info.js` derives the identity **at startup from the deployed sources** — a 12-hex sha256 fingerprint over `src/**` + `package.json` — with `BUILD_ID`/`GIT_SHA` overriding it when a real build pipeline supplies one, and `identitySource` stating which of the two it is (only an explicit id is traceable to a CI artifact, so the difference is reported rather than blurred). It reaches the three surfaces an operator actually uses: `/health` (additively — `{status,db,time}` unchanged), a `schoolos_build_info{id,version,fingerprint,env,node} 1` Prometheus gauge (label values escaped for the exposition format, since a malformed line makes Prometheus reject the whole scrape), and a post-`listen` boot-log line. **Derived rather than hand-maintained, and that is the load-bearing choice:** a typed version string is the same defect as the hand-typed test counts in §6.5 item 29 — true once, then silently false — so the test *mutates a copy of the tree and requires the fingerprint to move*, which fails if anyone ever replaces the computation with a constant. `tests/build-info.test.js` 5/5; mutation-probed by making `sourceFingerprint` return a constant → **4 of 5 fail**; restored 5/5 with zero `MUTANT` markers. |
| OPS-12 | P2 | Metrics are per-process and **arithmetically wrong with >1 replica** (counters persisted with `Math.max`), the rate limiter is in-memory with no shared store, and the 30s permission-binding cache is process-local — so revocation windows and brute-force budgets diverge per replica. | `server/src/app.js:19, 28-35, 996-1006`; `server/src/routes.js:129-139` | Per-pod label sum; shared limiter store; explicit revocation window via `LISTEN/NOTIFY` |
| OPS-13 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | Long-lived services have a 30-second stop grace, and `/ready` returns 503 while `app.schoolosDraining` is true so load balancers stop sending new work during shutdown. | `server/docker-compose.yml`; `server/src/app.js:/ready`; `server/tests/ops-deploy.test.js` | Keep drain/readiness coverage in deployment tests |
| OPS-14 | P2 | CI never builds or runs the artifact: no build/push/deploy job, no `docker compose up` smoke, and CI runs Node 22 while the image ships Node 26 — which is precisely how OPS-1/OPS-2 survive a green pipeline. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:9,40,66,78,84`; `server/Dockerfile:3,8` | Add a fresh-volume compose smoke job asserting `/ready`, plus build + version alignment |
| OPS-15 | P2 | The comms dispatcher holds row locks across sequential network calls (up to 20 rows × 8s inside one transaction against a 5s `lock_timeout` and 60s idle-in-transaction timeout). | `server/scripts/comms-dispatcher.js:163-183`; `server/src/db.js:10,15` | Claim, commit, then call providers outside the transaction |
| OPS-16 | **P3 — CLOSED 2026-09-16** | Request IDs are returned on responses and error bodies; nightly backups include verified private file storage; and restore now replays encrypted DPDP erasure tokens before traffic is enabled. | `server/src/app.js`; `server/scripts/backup.sh`; `server/scripts/replay-erasure-journal.js`; `server/migrations/094_erasure_replay_token.sql` | Keep the restore drill in the quarterly runbook |

| OPS-17 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-14 (`app.js` duplicate-series guard + `tests/ops-deploy.test.js`)** | Found while fixing OPS-3, and not in this register: **`/metrics` emitted a duplicate series**, so `schoolos_requests_total`/`schoolos_errors_total` appeared twice in one scrape once the 60s flush had written `app.ops_counters` — both names are exactly what that flush persists, and the endpoint exported `schoolos_<name>` for every row *after* already emitting both from the in-process counters. Prometheus rejects a scrape containing two samples for one series ("duplicate sample for timestamp"), so the ONE endpoint any alerting must be built on failed outright **in the steady state** while looking healthy in the first minute after boot. Fixed by reserving those two names for the live values; every other counter is still exported (worker heartbeats depend on that path, and the test asserts it rather than assuming). | `server/src/app.js` (`/metrics` assembly, `ops_counters` flush); verified live in `tests/ops-deploy.test.js` — reverting the guard fails the test with "duplicate series in one scrape" |
| OPS-18 | **P1 — CLOSED 2026-09-15 (ownership fix + static gate; build still unproven, which is OPS-14)** | `Dockerfile:12-14` did a plain `COPY src src` / `COPY scripts scripts` and `:18` sets `USER node`. `COPY` preserves source modes, and **15 of 19 files in `server/scripts/` are mode `600`**, including `outbox-worker.js`, `comms-dispatcher.js`, `retention-worker.js`, `webhook-worker.js` and `workflow-worker.js`; in `server/src/` **`index.js` and `config.js` are `600`**. If that held in the image, the `api` container could not read `src/index.js` and no worker could read its entry script. | measured file modes on disk; `server/Dockerfile` | **CLOSED — by OWNERSHIP, not by `chmod`, and the distinction is the fix.** Every runtime source `COPY` is now `COPY --chown=node:node`, so the runtime user OWNS the files and a mode-600 source is readable. `chmod 644` was rejected as the primary fix because **git records only the executable bit**: 600 and 644 both checkout as 644 under a normal umask, so a repository permission normalisation would not survive the next clone — the defect would silently return on any machine with a strict umask. `--chown` is enforced at build time from the context's actual modes, which is the only place they are visible. Pinned by new `server/tests/ops-dockerfile.test.js` (3/3), which asserts the non-root `USER`, that every runtime-source `COPY` carries `--chown=node:node`, and that no second unguarded `COPY src`/`COPY scripts` shadows the fix. **Honest limit:** this is a static gate; it does NOT prove a container boots (that is OPS-14 — CI still never builds the image). |
| OPS-19 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-15 (already fixed in compose; the row was stale, now pinned)** | The `retention` one-shot declares no `healthcheck:` while the image-level HEALTHCHECK probes `:8080/ready` — a port no worker listens on — so `docker compose ps` showed it unhealthy after every run. `outbox` and `comms-dispatcher` already disable theirs with a comment about killing exactly this noise. **Fixed this round** (healthcheck disabled); listed because the register has no row for it. | `server/docker-compose.yml`; `server/Dockerfile:17` | **CLOSED — the compose fix (`healthcheck: disable: true` on `retention`) had already landed; this row was stale, which is the register's recurring failure mode.** Re-verified against the file and pinned so it cannot regress: the OPS-13/OPS-19 block in `server/tests/ops-deploy.test.js` asserts retention disables its healthcheck. |
| OPS-20 | **P2 — CLOSED 2026-09-16 (named owner path + the event spine; pinned behaviourally)** | `dunning-reminders.js` and `reconcile-payments.js` each built a raw `pg.Client` from `DATABASE_URL` — the `schoolos` OWNER, which carries `BYPASSRLS` — with no `SET LOCAL ROLE schoolos_app`, i.e. the least-privilege violation `docs/PROJECT-MAP.md:414` records as item 48. Separately `reconcile-payments.js` wrote `app.events` directly instead of going through `emitEvent`, so its discrepancy findings had no subscriber. **CLOSED:** both workers now use the NAMED maintenance path (`sysq`/`tx` from `src/db.js`, no raw client), and reconciliation emits through `emitEvent` with `severity:'warn'`, `emitted_by:'worker:reconcile-payments'` and a `payment:<id>` subject. **The module is also importable now** (an `isMain` guard replaced the top-level sweep + `process.exit`), which is what makes the claim testable: `tests/next10-followups.test.js` plants a ledger↔rail discrepancy and asserts the resultant event carries the right severity and subject, and the reader grep asserts no `new pg.Client`/`pool.connect` remains. | `scripts/dunning-reminders.js`, `scripts/reconcile-payments.js`; `server/tests/next10-followups.test.js` |

_pending integration_

## 6. The last 10%, itemised

This is the part you asked for. It is ordered by **what unblocks what**, not by severity alone — fixing 6.1 makes 6.3 verifiable, and 6.2 is where the customer-visible pain lives.

### 6.1 Trust blockers — do not onboard a paying school before these

| # | Item | Why it is on this list | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Stop persisting the reset token** — always redact the ledger body; strip `body` from `GET /communications` | A `coordinator`/`finance`/`transport`/`principal`/`itadmin` account can currently take over the owner's account. This is the single highest-severity item in the review. | a few hours |
| 2 | **Give the pool role its own non-owner login with `NOBYPASSRLS`** + `FORCE RLS` on the 40 tables that lack it | Until this lands, RLS protects one code path and tenant isolation is a coding convention. Every future door is one forgotten `WHERE tenant_id=` away from a cross-tenant leak. | 1–2 days |
| 3 | **Cap custom-role `allow` masks** to a subset of the authoring caller's own doors; forbid `base_role:'owner'` for itadmin; audit mask grants | Today an itadmin can mint a role granting every door including owner-only ones. | 1 day |
| 4 | **Re-state every ACL revoke in a new migration and revoke the stray `postgres` default ACL**; add `has_table_privilege` + `pg_default_acl` assertions to CI | The live DB grants the app role DELETE on 12 tables (including both Razorpay money ledgers) and UPDATE on `app.users` — contradicting two migrations whose checksums prove they ran. This is also your own D-35 law being violated in production. | 1 day |
| 5 | **Gate the three unguarded data doors**: `GET /attendance` (SEC-3), `POST /cases` (SEC-4), `/fees/obligations` fallback (SEC-5), `GET /events/lineage` (API-1) | Each returns whole-tenant data to students or parents. Small, surgical changes with an existing sibling door to copy. | half a day |
| 6 | **Validate plugin settings on the persisting door**; never return `settings` verbatim; encrypt secret keys | The settings column drives webhook secrets and gateway keys and accepts any shape. | 1 day |
| 7 | **Make the audit trail complete and tamper-evident**: hash inside `audit_append`, add `GET /audit/verify`, and log the currently-unaudited privileged mutations (attendance register, class transfer, payment confirm, admission→student, vault lifecycle, MFA state) | You cannot claim a hash-chained ledger while the hash is caller-supplied, nothing verifies it, and the register — a legal record — has no per-student row. | 3–5 days |
| 8 | **Fail closed on webhook timestamps**; derive `webhookVerified` from a real gateway record, not a regex; verify HMAC *before* resolving the tenant | Money provenance must be evidence, not a string shape. | 1 day |

### 6.2 Reachability blockers — why the features feel absent

| # | Item | Why it is on this list | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | **Build the organisation model**: `app.classes`, `app.subjects`, `app.teaching_assignments`, plus `UNIQUE (tenant_id, id)` on parents and composite FKs | This is §3, the keystone. It is what lets teachers mark attendance, gives every privacy rule a class-of-record boundary, and gives custom roles something to scope *to*. | 1–2 weeks |
| 10 | **Make `class`/`section`/`subject` real references** and backfill from the free-text denormals | 375 students currently carry free-text grade/section; nothing can be joined reliably. | part of #9 |
| 11 | **Add `librarian`** (+ `counselor`, and the other narrow roles from §5.2) and narrow the 9 over-granting plugins | The librarian account you asked for, and the safeguarding surface that currently admits the subject teachers it promises to exclude. | 2–3 days |
| 12 | **Make custom-role masks work on plugin doors** — one shared authorizer for both gates | 173 of 214 doors ignore the only real least-privilege mechanism you have. | 1–2 days |
| 13 | **Make the librarian preset an acceptance test** | The product ships the preset; it must pass end-to-end in CI or the feature is a promise, not a feature. | hours |
| 14 | **Serve `GET /me/effective`** (role, capabilities, scope, nav) and drive nav, palette and every screen from it; make screens degrade the way `Audit.tsx` already does | Today adding a role or plugin requires a frontend release, and the client invents its own permission model from 43 hardcoded prefixes and 12 nav branches. | 1–2 weeks |

### 6.3 Delivery blockers — features that exist but never run

| # | Item | Why it is on this list | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 15 | **Wire real outbound channels**: enqueue comms instead of returning `{sent:true}`; make `logger` non-default outside dev and refuse to claim success without a provider receipt | OTP, absence alerts, parent notices and dunning are no-ops. This is the product's core promise. | 3–5 days |
| 16 | **Add the three rails to the catalog and gate reset on settings presence, not row presence** | Password reset is 503 forever for every tenant. | hours |
| 17 | **Fix the relay**: a durable subscriber (or ack-based marking) instead of `console.log` + mark-processed; a high-water-mark cursor for webhook discovery instead of a 1-hour window; a parked counter separate from pending | The "at-least-once" spine is at-most-once to a log line, and events older than a 1-hour outage are silently never delivered. | 3–5 days |
| 18 | **Schedule the five unscheduled workers** + a last-run heartbeat per worker | Webhooks, workflow rules, dunning and reconciliation never execute in a real deployment. | 1 day |
| 19 | **Fix the compose boot order** (`migrate && bootstrap`) and pass the admin URL; add a fresh-volume smoke job to CI | The only documented production start path cannot boot an empty database, and CI cannot see it because CI never builds the artifact. | 1 day |
| 20 | **Schedule backups, alert on backup age, encrypt, and fix permissions**; include global roles in the dump | The README's "RPO ≤ 24h" holds only if an operator wires it by hand, and existing dumps cannot restore their own policies. | 1–2 days |

### 6.4 Platform, commercial and operations gaps

| # | Item | Why it is on this list | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 21 | **Enforce `permissions[]` at request time** and record the real manifest as the consent grant; stop accepting client-supplied `version`/`class`/`permissions` | The manifest is documented as a security contract and is currently decoration — the ceiling the whole plugin model rests on. | 3–5 days |
| 22 | **Add a tenant plan / entitlement check** with a 402 path | You cannot sell the product you built. | 2–3 days |
| 23 | **Open the integration surface**: extend API-key scopes to a documented read plane | No customer can integrate anything. | 3–5 days |
| 24 | **Alerting and SLOs**: queue-age, parked count, error rate, auth failures, webhook signature failures, backup age, worker heartbeats + a machine-readable metrics auth path | There is no alerting of any kind, so the event spine can die in silence. | 3–5 days |
| 25 | **Observability**: per-route duration histogram, `pg_stat_statements`, error tracking, commit/build id in `/health`, request id returned to clients | The documented p95 SLO is unmeasurable and "which build is live?" has no answer. | 2–3 days |
| 26 | **Add the missing indexes** (12 composite + 15 FK-leading) and enum domains / NOT NULLs | RLS appends `tenant_id` to every predicate, so these are scans per request. | 1–2 days |
| 27 | **Lifecycle: uninstall with export window, suspend, upgrade, rollback, tenant offboarding** | The docs promise all of it; only enable/disable exist. | 1 week |
| 28 | **API contract**: schemas + OpenAPI on the read plane, one shared `bad()`, `ERROR_CODES`, pagination envelope, `Idempotency-Key`, request timeouts | An integrator currently cannot write a retry, a page loop, or branch on an error type. | 1–2 weeks |

### 6.5 Instrumentation and honesty — cheap, and it changes how you manage the rest

| # | Item | Why it is on this list | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 29 | **Make the reported numbers generated artifacts** (test count, door count, coverage, permission matrix) stamped with commit + log path; delete hand-written pass counts | The release certificate claims 114/114 against an actual 392 tests, and the only full-run log is red. You cannot finish the last 10% while the instrument panel is wrong. | hours |
| 30 | **Fix the calendar bomb** (`fees-dunning` goes red 2026-10-06) and remove `Math.random()` from the seed — **`Math.random()` DONE 2026-09-13 (§8): seeded mulberry32, seed now reproducible; the 2026-10-06 dunning expiry is still armed** | The suite has an expiry date ~3 weeks out. | hours |
| 31 | **Replace the 8 tautological assertions and 10 conditional silent passes** | They inflate the pass count and hide real regressions. | 1–2 days |
| 32 | **Wire the missing gates**: lint in CI, coverage floor, migration-drift step, `--enforce` parity gate, Node version alignment, frontend tests (vitest) | A regression currently merges freely; 44k LOC of frontend has zero tests. | 1 week |
| 33 | **Test the untested critical components**: outbox relay, migration runner, backup/restore, plugin contract, concurrency on money/auth paths, and a cross-tenant sweep over the ~25 highest-PII doors | These are the components whose failure is discovered during an incident. | 1–2 weeks |
| 34 | **Fix the stale docs and delete the contradictions**: role counts, route counts, policy/table counts, the four mutually inconsistent plugin counts, the four already-fixed defects presented as open, the "10 pre-configured roles" copy inside the product, and the fabricated infrastructure telemetry in the ItAdmin screen | Documentation that disagrees with the code is worse than none, and customers can see some of it. | 2–3 days |

## 7. Execution plan

Ordered by leverage. Each phase has an exit criterion that is **testable** — if it cannot be verified by a command or a test, it is not done.

### Phase 0 — Stop the bleeding (1 week)
Items 1–8. **Exit:** a regression test proves a `PASSWORD_RESET` comms body never contains a token; `has_table_privilege('schoolos_app', …, 'DELETE')` is false for all money ledgers and asserted in CI; a student token gets 403 on `/attendance`, `/events/lineage`, `/fees/obligations`; `schoolos` is not a table owner and has no `BYPASSRLS`; an itadmin-authored `* /*` mask is rejected.

### Phase 1 — Make the instrument panel honest (3–4 days, do it in parallel with Phase 0)
Items 29–31. **Exit:** every number in `docs/` is generated from the runner; `npm test` prints a real count; the dunning tests pass on a clock 60 days ahead; a deliberately tautological test fails the gate.
*Rationale: this is the cheapest phase and it makes every later phase measurable. Right now you are managing a project with a broken dashboard — that is a large part of why the last 10% feels un-nailable.*

### Phase 2 — The keystone: the organisation model (2–3 weeks)
Items 9–13. **Exit:** a teacher can mark their own class's register and gets 403 for another class; the `librarian` role exists, the shipped preset passes end-to-end in CI, and a `facilities` account can no longer issue books or read guardian phones; a wellbeing/safeguarding screen admits `counselor` and excludes subject teachers; every custom-role mask is enforced on plugin doors (proved by a deny-mask test).
*Rationale: this is §3. It unblocks items 11–14, gives RBAC something to scope to, and removes the cause of the attendance and class-of-record defects. Doing RBAC work before this means doing it twice.*

### Phase 3 — Actually deliver (2 weeks)
Items 15–20. **Exit:** a reset email/SMS is recorded with a real provider receipt; `/auth/otp/request` cannot return `sent:true` without a dispatch; an event published while the webhook worker is down for 2 hours is delivered on recovery; every worker has a heartbeat and an alert; `docker compose up` on an empty volume reaches `/ready` 200; a backup restores into a scratch DB with its roles intact.

### Phase 4 — Make it a platform (2–3 weeks)
Items 21–23, 27. **Exit:** a plugin requesting a permission it was not granted gets a hard error plus an audit row; enabling an unentitled plugin returns 402; a school's read-only API key can pull students/attendance/fees; a plugin can be uninstalled with an export window and suspended.

### Phase 5 — Make it operable (1–2 weeks)
Items 24–26. **Exit:** an alert fires when the outbox has an unprocessed event older than 5 minutes or a backup is older than 26 hours; `/metrics` is reachable by a machine identity; a request id returned to the user appears in the logs; p95 per route is measured.

### Phase 6 — Make it integrable and honest (2–3 weeks)
Items 28, 32–34. **Exit:** OpenAPI published for the read plane with an error-code catalogue and a pagination envelope; frontend has a test runner with a route smoke suite; the cross-tenant sweep runs over the top 25 PII doors; `docs/` contains no claim that the code does not support.

### Definition of 100
The project is finished — not "90% with an asterisk" — when all of the following are true and machine-checked:

1. **No severity-P0 finding remains open** in this register.
2. **Every door's authorization is a capability decision**, not a role enum plus a hand-written check, and custom-role masks are enforced uniformly across kernel and plugin doors.
3. **Every tenant-scoped query runs under RLS with a non-bypassing role**, and CI fails on any query that does not.
4. **Every mutation writes an audit row in the same transaction**, and the chain is verifiable by a job that recomputes it.
5. **Every outbound message has a provider receipt**, and no code path reports success without one.
6. **Every privileged mutation is idempotent or accepts an idempotency key.**
7. **One command boots a fresh environment and reaches `/ready`**, and one command proves a backup restores.
8. **Every number in `docs/` is generated from the code**, and the docs contain no claim the code does not support.
9. **A teacher, a librarian, a counsellor and a parent can each do their whole job** with the account the product says they should have.
10. **A paying school can be onboarded, entitled, integrated with, alerted on, and offboarded** without an engineer touching the database.

## Appendix A — First-hand verification performed by the lead auditor

These were reproduced directly against the live code and database rather than taken from any report. Each is cited so it can be re-checked.

| # | Fact verified | Evidence | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | The library plugin's write doors admit `['owner','principal','coordinator','facilities']` — there is no librarian concept, so a facilities staffer can issue and return books and the principal is the de-facto librarian. | `server/src/plugins/library/index.js:31` | The customer's headline complaint, confirmed in code |
| A2 | Plugin navigation is filtered by **tenant enablement only**, never by role: `useKernelNav` returns items where `isEnabled(plugin)`; `KernelGate` gates routes on enablement; the only role check in the plugin surface is `canTurnOn` (owner/itadmin). | `app/src/plugins/useKernel.tsx:130-165` | Once Library is enabled, its menu entry and route are visible to every signed-in user of that tenant, including students and parents |
| A3 | The plugin registry has **no role/permission field at all** for nav or route visibility. | `app/src/plugins/pluginRegistry.ts:55-84` | There is no mechanism in the product to say "this screen belongs to a librarian" |
| A4 | Catalog↔registry drift on the library plugin: backend catalog says `class 'C'`, permissions `["students.read","events.emit","audit.request"]`; the frontend registry says `class 'A'`, permissions `['students.read','events.emit','actions.raise','audit.request']`. | `server/src/plugin-catalog.js:23` vs `app/src/plugins/pluginRegistry.ts:70-83` | The "class" (blast radius) and the declared capability set of the same plugin disagree between client and server |
| A5 | Approving a communication action writes `status='delivered'`, `sent_at=now()`, `delivered_at=now()` into `app.communications` and emits an event labelled "Parent notice SENT — …" **without calling any rail**. | `server/src/routes.js:958-966` | The product, and the audit ledger, assert a parent notice was delivered when nothing left the building |
| A6 | The SMS rail's default provider is `logger`, and its "send" path returns `{ ok: true, provider: 'logger' }` having sent nothing. | `server/src/plugins/rails.sms/index.js:9-14, 81-90` | The rail's own contract conflates "logged" with "sent", so no caller can distinguish them — this is the mechanism behind A5 |
| A7 | The role taxonomy is duplicated in four places: DB CHECK constraint `users_role_check` (12 roles), `server/src/auth.js:60`, `app/src/types/domain.ts:10` (`RoleId`), and hardcoded per-feature allowlists in `app/src/state/store.tsx:251-253`. | psql `pg_constraint` on `app.users`; `server/src/auth.js:60`; `app/src/types/domain.ts:10`; `app/src/state/store.tsx:251-253` | Adding a `librarian` role is a 4-file, 2-layer change today, and the copies can silently disagree |
| A8 | `force_change` **is** enforced now, via a restricted session stamped on the shared issue path. | `server/src/app.js:139-143, 217, 257` | Corrects the older docs, which call it "stamped-but-unenforced" |
| A9 | MFA doors exist (`/me/mfa/enroll`, `/me/mfa/disable`, `/me/mfa/verify`), rate-limited. | `server/src/app.js:608, 625, 643` | Corrects `STILL NOT GOOD_2` §4, which says "MFA … unchanged" |
| A10 | Fee-plan creation **does** exist now (`/fee_plan_templates` create + obligation generation with a real audit row). | `server/src/routes.js:511-558` | Corrects `STILL NOT GOOD_2` §4 ("still no door anywhere") |
| A11 | The session JWT is stored in `localStorage` (read at boot, written on login). | `app/src/services/liveApi.ts:19, 33, 292` | Any XSS is a full-account takeover; there is no httpOnly cookie path |
| A12 | A `/capabilities` door exists, but it reports **feature availability** (rails configured, plugins enabled), not what the signed-in user may do. | `server/src/app.js:851-880`; `app/src/services/capabilitiesApi.ts:1-40` | The decoupling contract the UI needs exists for "is it configured", not for "am I allowed" — so permissions stay hardcoded client-side (see A7) |
| A13 | **The owner-takeover chain, verified link by link**: the reset mail body embeds the raw token; the *hashed* token goes to `auth.reset_tokens`; the **plaintext** token goes into `app.communications.body`, redacted **only when `!r.sandbox`**; `sandbox:true` is returned by the default `logger` provider *and* by every failure path; `GET /communications` runs `SELECT *` and admits six roles; the request door is unauthenticated. | `server/src/app.js:515, 547, 552-563`; `plugins/rails.email/index.js:145, 152, 154`; `server/src/routes.js:1356-1360` | A non-owner staff role can take over the owner account with no cryptography broken — the most severe finding in the review |
| A14 | The API pool connects as **`schoolos`, which owns the tables and has `rolbypassrls = t`**; the RLS role `schoolos_app` correctly has `rolbypassrls = f`. In the schemas I sampled, 66 tables have RLS enabled and 53 are `FORCE`d. | live `pg_roles`, `pg_class` | RLS applies only on the `withUser` path; every bare `q()` query is unconstrained by policy |
| A15 | **Live ACLs contradict the migrations**: `schoolos_app` holds `DELETE` on **12** tables (including `plugin_razorpay.order_log` and `plugin_razorpay.webhook_log`), `UPDATE` on `app.users` (which holds `password_hash` and `role`) and on `app.enrollment_history`, and `SELECT` on `app.webhook_subscriptions` (whose `sign_secret` signs every outbound webhook for a tenant) — while a **stray `postgres`-owned default ACL** `{schoolos_app=arwd/postgres}` grants full DML including DELETE on every future `app` table, outside the repo and outside the migration checksum ledger. | live `has_table_privilege`, `information_schema.role_table_grants`, `pg_default_acl`; `migrations/054_security_sprint.sql:71-72, 130-137` | The project's own D-35 law ("the app role NEVER receives table-level DELETE in schema app") is violated in production, and the landmine re-creates the violation for every new table |
| A16 | `GET /attendance` scopes the `parent` branch via `family.child_link` but has **no `student` branch**, so a student falls through to the whole-school query and reads every pupil's name, admission number, grade, section and daily status (`LIMIT 2000`). | `server/src/routes.js:355-376` (vs `:286`, `:317`) | Whole-school child data readable by a child account |
| A17 | `POST /cases` is gated on bare `me` with **no role check**, while its sibling read (`GET /cases`) and status (`POST /cases/:id/status`) doors both enforce `STAFF_CASE_ROLES` — and the body lets the caller set `priority`, `owner` and `slaHours`. | `server/src/routes.js:811` vs `:789-791, 826-828` | Students and parents can inject fabricated critical work into the staff board and the event spine |
| A18 | `GET /events/lineage` is gated on bare `me` with no role filter, while the sibling `GET /events` requires ten staff roles. The returned `label` fields embed other families' data (amounts, child names, bookings). | `server/src/routes.js:1394` vs `:1332` | The tenant's whole activity stream — including money and safeguarding categories — is readable by students and parents |
| A19 | The custom-role mask can grant **every** door: `MASK_RE` accepts `* /*`, `validateMask` only requires each pattern to match *some* route, `require()` treats an `allow` match as sufficient to lift a base-role refusal, and `ROLES_ADMIN` is `['owner','itadmin']`. | `server/src/routes.js:84, 101-124, 163, 24` | An itadmin — not owner-equivalent — can mint a grant-all role; audit rows still show the base role |
| A20 | **The shipped "Librarian" preset cannot work.** Its own comment names it as the acceptance case for the customer's request; `base_role:'teacher'` but `LibraryLive.tsx:26` gates every control on `['owner','principal','coordinator','facilities']`, so the librarian sees the module and none of its controls; and its six `/plugins/library/*` mask grants are validated then never consulted because `requireRole` never calls `applyCustomMask`. The server's own `STAFF` list excludes `teacher` too, so the door refuses as well. | `app/src/screens/RolesAdmin.tsx:64-83`; `app/src/screens/LibraryLive.tsx:26, 116, 144, 199`; `server/src/app.js:783-790`; `plugins/library/index.js:31` | The exact feature requested fails at both layers, and the preset is the product's own acceptance criterion |
| A21 | Any role string outside the 12-item list is silently mapped to **`principal`** — the broadest navigation — rather than the narrowest. | `app/src/App.tsx:180-182` | Provisioning errors look like a working principal instead of an error; the nav switch has no `default` branch, so this fallback is load-bearing |
| A22 | Live registry state confirms the platform's own instruments misread: `SELECT DISTINCT owner_role FROM app.actions` returns only `principal` (so the disable-drain matches zero rows), `SELECT DISTINCT emitted_by FROM app.events` returns only `kernel` (so per-plugin budgets cannot trip), and live plugin manifests are **41–43 byte stubs** for migration-seeded rows. | live `app.actions`, `app.events`, `pg_column_size(manifest)` per plugin row | Several "working" controls are provably inert in the deployed database |
| A23 | The Fastify instance sets no `requestTimeout`/`connectionTimeout` against a single `max: 20` pool, and `zod` is declared in `package.json` but imported nowhere. | `server/src/app.js:24`; `server/src/db.js:7`; grep `from 'zod'` → 0 hits | One hung outbound call can exhaust the pool and take down the whole API; the declared validation dependency is phantom |

---

## Appendix B — Method and limits

### How this audit was produced

Nine areas were assessed by dedicated reading lanes, each instructed to work **read-only** — no file writes, no git, no migrations or seeds executed — and to cite a `file:line` for every claim. Five lanes were security-focused and ran in sequence, each one revising the previous score when it found or disproved something; that revision history is preserved in §5.1 because it is itself evidence of how much depth changes the answer.

**What each lane did beyond reading source, where it could:**
- **Live database interrogation** (`pg_roles`, `pg_class`, `pg_policies`, `pg_default_acl`, `pg_proc`, `has_table_privilege`, `information_schema`, `app.schema_migrations`, row and size counts) — this is what converted several "documented control" claims into "control state proven otherwise", and what found the ACL divergence and the stub manifests.
- **Parsing rather than grepping**: all 578 template-literal query arguments were parsed with a balanced-literal parser to test for SQL injection (result: none — 550 `$N`-bound, 25 fully static, 3 constants or server-derived probes). The same technique was applied to path handling for traversal (result: none).
- **Empirical RLS probes**: cross-tenant `SELECT count(*)` as `schoolos_app` with a foreign tenant GUC (0 rows) and with the GUC unset (0 rows — fail-closed), which is how the *good* RLS news was established.
- **End-to-end exploit chains**, traced from an unauthenticated entry point to a privileged outcome. Two survived scrutiny (the reset-token takeover; the itadmin mask escalation) and one did not (the optional webhook timestamp could not be turned into a replay, because event-id dedupe already covers it — so it is reported as a false claim, not an invented attack).

**Lead-auditor verification.** I did not take any load-bearing claim on trust. Every item in Appendix A was reproduced by me directly — including the full reset-token chain, the live ACL state, the three unguarded doors, the mask-escalation primitives, and the librarian preset. Where a lane's claim could not be reproduced, it does not appear in this document.

### Limits — what this audit does not prove

1. **No test suite was run during this audit**, and no load, fuzz or penetration testing was performed. Findings about race conditions (API-3, API-13, DAT-13) and about the test suite's own defects (TST-*) are established by reading code and by the suite's own recorded logs, not by executing it.
2. **The live database examined is a development instance** with 2 tenants and 16 users. `student.*`, `teacher.note`, `ptm.*`, `plugin_clinic.visit` and `app.outbox` hold **zero rows**, so those RLS policies were validated by catalogue inspection rather than with data. Cross-tenant and leak findings were proved with `SELECT count(*)` / role probes only.
3. **Git was out of scope** by project policy. Consequences: the exposed `JWT_SECRET` and webhook secrets in `server/.env` are rated **P1 rather than P0** because disclosure via history could not be established. Whether the historical test logs correspond to the current tree is likewise unverifiable.
4. **Four claims are explicitly labelled INFERENCE** in the register and are not laundered into fact: Postgres's omitted-`WITH CHECK` fallback semantics; the *mechanism* behind the surviving DELETE grants (the control state is proven, the cause is not); gateway payload shapes and 429 retry policy; and whether `server/.env` was ever committed.
5. **Two area scores are the lead auditor's own assessment** rather than a dedicated lane's: product completeness (§5.6) and frontend coherence (§1.2 row 7). Both are grounded in the cited evidence of the other lanes; their provenance is flagged so it can be challenged separately.
6. **Scores are judgements, not measurements.** The weights in §1.2 are stated so you can disagree with them and recompute — if you believe product breadth deserves a heavier weight than trust, the total moves. What is not a judgement is the findings register, which is evidence.
7. **Documentation staleness cuts both ways, and I only resolved it in one direction.** Where the code is *better* than the docs claim (MFA doors, fee-plan doors, `force_change`, the bootstrap grant matrix, the removed AI telemetry row), I record the code as correct. The reverse — a doc claiming a control the code lacks — is reported as a finding rather than a score deduction, because the honest statement is "this claim is false", not "this feature is missing".

---

## 8. Change log — what has been closed since the audit

This section is the running record of work done against this document. Each entry names the finding it closes, the file it changed, and the **test that will fail if it regresses** — because a fix without a failing-test is a claim, not a fix.

### SEC-0 — owner takeover via the persisted reset token · **CLOSED 2026-09-13**
- **Change.** The reset mail body is now redacted on **every** path, not only when `!r.sandbox`: `server/src/app.js:552-570`. The ledger is delivery *evidence*; it is no longer a delivery *channel*. The former `r.sandbox ? body : body.replace(raw, …)` branch was the whole vulnerability, because `sandbox:true` is the permanent default for any tenant without SMTP mail (`rails.email/index.js:145,152,154`).
- **Dev usability preserved, not sacrificed.** Local developers previously recovered the token *from the ledger*. That affordance now goes to stdout instead, behind its own door that mirrors the existing T101 pattern (development **and** explicit opt-in): `RESET_TOKEN_DEV_LOG`, computed in `server/src/config.js:80-93`. No production config can arm it, and nothing tenant-readable is involved either way.
- **Regression pin.** `server/tests/l2-rails-email-reset.test.js` — the suite previously *scraped the raw token out of `app.communications.body`* and asserted the ledger "carries the raw token", i.e. **the tests codified the vulnerability**. It now mints its own token via a `mintToken()` helper and asserts the inverse: a live token exists (`auth.reset_tokens` +1) **and** no 64-hex credential appears in the ledger body, on the **failure** path as well as the success path (the failure path also persisted a body, and a token that never reached a human is still a live credential — this closes a gap the old suite noted in a comment and left unasserted).
- **Verified.** `TEST_DB_NAME=schoolos_l3_reset node --test tests/l2-rails-email-reset.test.js` → **6 pass, 0 fail**, including the pre-existing single-use, expiry, epoch-revocation, anti-enumeration and no-channel-503 assertions, none of which were weakened.
- **Deliberately not done.** SEC-0's optional hardening — giving `/communications` an explicit column list that omits `body` — is **not** applied. No frontend or backend consumer reads that column (verified by grep across `app/src` and `server/src`), so removing it would be an API-contract change with no security benefit now that no credential can reach the table, and it risks breaking an external consumer. The invariant is enforced at the write site and pinned by test instead. Revisit only if a future need puts a secret back on this path.
- **Residual risk, stated plainly.** If any *future* code writes a credential into a comms body, this fix does not catch it. The class-level guard is the §7 item that makes "success without a provider receipt" structurally impossible; until that lands, the redaction is enforced per-path.

### SEC-3 — a student could read the whole-school attendance register · **CLOSED 2026-09-13**
- **Change.** `GET /attendance` scoped only the `parent` branch, so **every other role — including `student`** — fell through to the whole-school query and read each pupil's `admission_no`, `name`, `grade`, `section`, `status` and `note` (`LIMIT 2000`). It now refuses `student` with the project's own refusal idiom, matching the sibling roster doors that already did this at `routes.js:286` and `:317`: `server/src/routes.js:355-365`.
- **Why it was a bug and not a policy.** Two doors in the same file already refused students for the same data; this one did not. That is drift, not intent — and the client's own 43-entry `staffOnly` list already assumed `/attendance` was staff-only, so the API was the only layer not enforcing it.
- **Parent scope untouched.** The `own_children` branch is unchanged and is asserted as part of the test, so the fix cannot silently over-restrict the one role that legitimately needs a scoped read.
- **Regression pin.** `server/tests/security.test.js` → *"SEC-3: a student cannot read the whole-school attendance register"*: asserts the student gets `403` with `staff-only` copy and **no `rows` key**, the teacher still gets `200`, and the parent still gets `200` with `scoped === 'own_children'`.
- **Honest refusal copy.** The message deliberately does **not** point the student at a self-service attendance door, because none exists — promising a route that does not exist is the same class of defect this document is about.

### API-1 — the tenant event stream was readable by students and parents · **CLOSED 2026-09-13**
