Least privilege at column granularity: table-level `UPDATE` on `app.users` narrowed to the **seven columns the code actually writes** (derived by enumerating every `UPDATE app.users` in the repo, each listed with file:line in the migration header); `app.enrollment_history` and `app.files` lost `UPDATE` entirely (both INSERT-only across the whole codebase); `app.webhook_subscriptions` lost table-level `SELECT`, so `sign_secret` — which signs every outbound webhook for a tenant — is unreadable by the app role. Verified live: table `UPDATE`=f, escalate-via-`role`=f, `active`/`password_hash`=t, `sign_secret`=f, non-secret columns=t.
  - The most valuable assertion is **functional, not a privilege bit**: it runs `UPDATE app.users SET active=…` **as the app role inside a real `withUser` transaction** (must succeed — otherwise suspending a user is broken) and `SET role='principal'` (must fail 42501). A blanket `REVOKE` would satisfy an ACL-shape assertion while breaking the product, so both directions are pinned.
  - **The test caught me asserting a privilege that does not exist**: I assumed the app role could `INSERT` into `app.webhook_subscriptions`. It cannot — creation runs on the **owner** path (`app.js:759` uses `sysq`), a *stronger* position than the audit asked for. The assertion now pins that reality instead of my assumption.
- **SEC-4 CLOSED — but deliberately NOT the way this document recommended, and that is the round's most important judgement.** The register says to gate `POST /cases` on `STAFF_CASE_ROLES` and force `origin:'staff'`. **That would have broken a deliberate, tested product behaviour:** `tests/cases-privacy.test.js` asserts a parent CAN file a complaint through that door with `origin:'parent'`, and its header states "POST /cases stays open to every signed-in role (parents must be able to report)". So the door stays open to families. The real defect is not *who* may open a case but *which fields the caller controls* — four staff decisions were surrendered to the request body: `origin` (a parent could label their own report 'staff'/'system', misattributing it in the audit spine), `owner` (assign a case to any named staff member), `priority` (self-declared `critical` at the top of the board) and `slaHours` (an SLA long enough that the case never escalates). All four are now **derived from the caller's role**; staff keep the full vocabulary (verified: a principal can still set critical + owner + a long SLA). Invalid priorities are now a 400 rather than the DB CHECK's 500.
- **SEC-5 CLOSED** — `/fees/obligations` returned up to 500 obligations for the **whole school** to any non-parent role, students included. Now staff-only (`owner, principal, itadmin, finance, coordinator` — deliberately narrower than `STAFF_CASE_ROLES`: no teacher, no admissions, because this is financial data about children). **Honest limitation, recorded in both the code and the test:** the audit's preferred fix ("student → own obligations only") is **not implementable** — there is no link from a user account to a student record anywhere in the schema (`app.users` has no `student_id`; the only linkage tables are *guardian* links). Non-staff are therefore refused rather than scoped: a missing feature beats a silent whole-school disclosure. Building that linkage is Phase 2.
- `tests/door-gating-sec4-sec5.test.js` (11/11) — pins the derived fields, the refusals, the unusable-priority 400, **and that the gate is not vacuous** (finance still reads the ledger; a parent is still scoped to their own children rather than refused).
- **Full suite: 448 tests, 447 pass, 0 fail, 0 cancelled, 1 explicit skip, EXIT=0.**
- After SEC-20 (below): **462 tests, 461 pass, 0 fail, 0 cancelled, 1 skip, EXIT=0** — the suite grew by the 14 new audit-integrity tests.
- **Two new P0s found by reconnaissance and registered (SEC-20, SEC-21), plus DAT-14.** SEC-20: `app.audit_append` accepts `p_hash` and stores it **verbatim** while validating only `p_prev_hash`, so any app-role caller can write a ledger row whose hash it chose — and **this is live, not theoretical**: recomputing all 53 live rows from their own columns reproduces only 27, and for one row the stored hash matches the *migration file's* `outcome` literal rather than the `outcome` the row now contains, i.e. it was altered after insertion. That also **corrects a claim in this document** (§5 said the chain "verifies clean live") — it measured *links*, not *hashes*. SEC-21: the Razorpay webhook door resolves a tenant and reads that tenant's settings **before** verifying the HMAC, and because its 403 is payload-dependent an unsigned request naming an existing order id returns 401 while an unknown one returns 403 — an unauthenticated existence oracle; both payment rails also **fail open** when the timestamp is absent. DAT-14: `workflow-worker.js` has DAT-4's one-hour window **plus** a `LIMIT 50` starvation bug the original audit missed.
- **Bookkeeping error of mine, corrected:** the row I added in the previous round for the policy-hardening hole reused an ID that was **already a P2 register item** (SEC-13), creating a duplicate. Renumbered to **SEC-17**. Caught by a failing assertion in an edit script, not by review.

### §8 — Independent review of the OPS-5/PRD-4 work found 8 defects (5 real, 3 gaps); RBAC-2 completed with the 13th role · **2026-09-13**

The full-suite verification deadlock from the previous round was resolved by giving review its own lane: a
read-only reviewer with its own log, told to work on my tree while a suite ran, no migrations, unique
`TEST_DB_NAME`, no git. It found eight things, and the distribution is the point — **the defect that broke CI
was not in the feature, it was in a SCRIPT the feature exposed.**

- **B3 (root cause, P1) — `scripts/seed.js` silently reverted the rails in every seeded environment.** It
  `TRUNCATE app.tenants CASCADE`s (which empties `app.plugins`) and then replays a **hard-coded migration list
  that stops at 048**. Migration 078's rows therefore vanished on every seed — every test database, the demo,
  CI. That is what made the OTP door 503 in CI and in dev (B1). Fixed at the level the drift lives at: the seed
  now **calls `ensurePluginCatalog`** for each tenant after the replay, the same function signup and the
  `GET /plugins` self-heal use, so the seed can never disagree with the catalog again. Pinned by asserting a
  freshly seeded database already contains all three rails **before** any self-heal runs.
- **B1 (P1, my regression)** — `/auth/otp/request` 503'd in the default posture, failing the DAT-6 gate in
  `tests/audit-coverage.test.js`. Fixed the honest way: the test now **configures the channel** (one plugin row,
  exactly what a school does) instead of the door being weakened, and no assertion was touched.
- **B2 (P1)** — *an oracle built out of the honest refusal*: the channel gate was asked only after a phone was
  matched to an account, so a school with no SMS channel answered 503 for a registered number and 200 for a
  stranger's. Scoped requests now answer the channel question **before** the lookup, so the refusal cannot
  depend on whose number it is, and the response body keeps a **constant key set** (`channel`/`provider`/
  `sandbox` present and null when nothing was sent) so its shape is not a second oracle. The unscoped residual
  is registered as **OPS-5b** rather than papered over.
- **B4 (P1)** — `rails.email` was **missing from the client plugin registry**, so the owner's Plugin Manager
  could not show or switch on the rail that the reset door now requires: the fix was reachable through the API
  (which is how it was verified) and **unreachable through the only owner-facing UI**. Mirror is load-bearing,
  not decoration.
- **B5 → PLG-14 (P1)** — the catalog seeded **schema-less manifests** (`{id, version}`), and the plugin manager
  reads its settings form out of the manifest: every tenant provisioned since the catalog landed showed
  "Nothing to configure" for all 29 plugins. Now resolves each plugin's own `manifest.js`, with a test that
  fails if any `CATALOG` entry lacks one. (My own first diagnostic was wrong here — I asserted `manifest.name`
  and reported seven "stub" plugins; `library`'s manifest legitimately has no `name` key. The false negative is
  why the fix now asserts *what the UI needs* — a settings schema — rather than a key I guessed at.)
- **B6 (P2)** — the OTP SMS went out with **no row in `app.communications`**, while the reset door it was
  modelled on writes one. Now recorded, with the one-time code **redacted** (the ledger is staff-readable; a
  recorded code is a usable credential), audience stored in E.164, `sent_at` set when the carrier took it and
  `delivered_at` only when one actually confirms.
- **B7 (P2)** — `body.devEcho` reported the **config flag**, so a response could claim `devEcho:true` while a
  rail carried the code and nothing was echoed. It now reports what happened.
- **S1 (P2, real)** — the door accepted `+`-less numbers while the rail accepts only E.164, so such a number
  was minted and then withdrawn on **every** attempt: permanently un-sign-in-able. Both doors now normalise to
  E.164 once, so the lookup key, the vault key, the verify-side key and the carrier address are the same string.
- **S2** — the gates accept only `status='enabled'` while other code accepts `enabled|active|degraded`. Left as
  is deliberately (a rail is either switched on or it is not) and recorded in the row text rather than "fixed"
  into a second meaning for the same word.
- **B8 → RBAC-1c (P2, OPEN)** — the mask ceiling cannot see `me`-gated doors, and the converted checks are
  asymmetric (read but not write). Registered with the exact trigger and the reviewer's honest scope note: a
  widening behind a blind guardrail, not a low-privilege escalation.

**RBAC-2 CLOSED — and the reason it took a round is worth recording.** I believed the fix was "add `counselor`
to `auth.js` ROLES". It was **three lists plus a schema default**: `auth.js` ROLES, the
`app.users.role`/`app.roles.base_role` **CHECK constraints** (migration 079 — without it my code change turned a
clean 400 into a **500 at the database**), and `people.admin`'s hand-copied list. The console list is now
**derived** from `ROLES` so the only declaration is one file, the CHECK makes a mismatch fail loudly, and
`tests/frontend-grants.test.js` fails if the client mirror drifts from the server list. Tiering: a
CONCERN_OVERSIGHT tier admits the counselor to the boards while **assignment** stays with the coordinator tier,
and safety-box readers narrowed from "every staff role except teacher" — which admitted finance, admissions,
transport, facilities and technician to a child's protection record — to the safeguarding tier. A counselor
reads the **assigned** queue (`?assigned=1`); the full box stays with the designated authority, which is this
door's own design and is now pinned by test rather than assumed.

- **`tests/rbac-counselor.test.js` (6/6), both halves mutation-verified** — removing the counselor from
  `CONCERN_OVERSIGHT` fails exactly the oversight assertion; removing it from the wellbeing readers fails
  exactly the wellbeing assertion (403 `role counselor not permitted`), and nothing else.
- **`tests/rails-catalog-otp.test.js` (9/9)** now also pins the seed invariant, the manifest resolution, the
  uniform refusal, the ledger row and the E.164 key.
- **The OTP door's own rate limit (5/minute) is a real constraint on tests**: my first version of these tests
  spent seven requests and failed on 429. Consolidated to four — the door was right and the test was greedy.

### §8 — Round 21: the full suite found what standalone runs could not, and CI had never actually been green · **2026-09-13**

Two independent delegations ran in parallel with my own work: a verifier re-attacking round 20, and a
reconnaissance pass over the two remaining P0s (PLG-1, PLG-2). The verifier found nine things and the full
suite found two more that no single-file run could — **both of the suite's findings were mine, and one of them
had been hiding a second defect behind it.**

- **The suite's first catch: my seed fix collided with test fixtures.** Making `scripts/seed.js` run the plugin
  catalog means `rails.email`/`rails.sms`/`rails.whatsapp` now EXIST for every seeded tenant. Eight test files
  insert plugin rows; six already did it idempotently, three did not — and a blind `INSERT` in
  `l2-rails-email-reset.test.js`'s `before()` died on `plugins_tenant_id_plugin_id_key`, which cancelled six
  tests and masked a **second** defect behind it: two message-copy regexes in that same file pinned the OLD
  reset copy that PRD-4 rewrote. Fixing the collision revealed the copy drift; both are now fixed, and the
  copy assertions still demand the same behaviour (a 503 that names the missing channel, never a fake `sent`).
- **The suite's second catch: a pinned privilege count, moved by a false positive — 110 vs 108.** The pin
  counts owner-path query sites in `src/`, and it was counting `export function bindKernel(sysq)` and
  `bindKernel(sysq)` in the rails: a parameter declaration and an argument, not queries. Two things were
  wrong at once: my `sysq` parameter name, and a test that could not tell an identifier from a call. Fixed at
  the source (the parameter is now `ownerQuery` — accurate: whatever owner-path query the caller binds) and the
  inventory re-measured and re-justified in place at 110, naming the two sites the rails work added and the one
  dead fallback read it removed. Tightening the regex to require a following quote was tried and **rejected**:
  29 real call sites put their SQL on the next line, so it under-reported at 79.
- **A stray backup file I had left in `src/`** (`src/.app-bk2.js`, 64 owner-path sites) was inflating the same
  count and would have been a second copy of `app.js` for the next reader to trip over. Deleted.
- **The verifier's P1, proved: my 503 refusals broke the "constant shape" claim.** Both OTP refusals returned
  `{error}` alone, so `channel`/`provider`/`sandbox` were absent exactly when a refusal happened — and the
  shipped test failed in the CI posture, where that branch actually runs. It was green on my machine only
  because `.env` arms `OTP_DEV_ECHO=1`, which skips the branch entirely. Fixed with one `refuse()` body that
  carries the same keys as a success (`sent:false`), and the test now asserts the key set rather than trusting
  it. **This is the third time independent verification has caught something my own runs could not see.**
- **CI had never been green.** `ci.yml` runs the whole suite with `NODE_ENV: test`; `config.js` arms the OTP dev
  echo only when `env === 'development'`, and `tests/l1-security-sprint.test.js` needs the issued code to probe
  vault isolation and tenant binding — so three tests in that file failed in CI regardless of my changes. I
  first "fixed" it by relaxing the guard to `env !== 'production'`, and **reverted that immediately**: T101
  pins "staging/test NODE_ENV never arms the flags" and it is right to. The honest fix is the opposite one — the
  file now declares the posture it models (`NODE_ENV='development'` before the dynamic app import, next to the
  `OTP_DEV_ECHO='1'` it already set), while T101 keeps probing the real postures in fresh processes. 23/23 in
  the CI posture.
- **Round 20's other verifier findings, fixed:** the one-time code was only PARTIALLY redacted in the comms
  ledger (`code.slice(0, 2)` left two of six digits in a table six staff roles can read — a 10^4 search space
  for a live credential); a safety-box report could be assigned to a role its own doors refuse (RBAC-2b); the
  provisioning screen could not offer the counsellor role RBAC-2 had just made real (a source-checked drift
  guard now covers it); and a phone stored as typed could never sign in (DAT-15).
- **Reconnaissance for the remaining P0s (PLG-1, PLG-2) is delivered** (`.coop/logs/recon-plg12.md`, 688
  lines): both confirmed, and worse than the register said — `scripts/comms-dispatcher.js` serves the email/SMS/
  WhatsApp rails with NO registry check at all, meridian's rails are `enabled` with an empty grant, 7 of 29
  manifests declare no permissions while their code calls `emitEvent`/`requestAudit`, and the permission
  vocabulary has drifted three ways (docs 10, catalog 13, client 15 — none a subset of another). Metering
  evidence measured: `app.events` has 7 rows, all `emitted_by='kernel'`; `app.ai_calls` is empty;
  `app.communications` has no `plugin_id` column — so no trustworthy per-plugin usage number exists today.
- **The honest state of Phase 1**: the instrument panel is still the phase it was — the eight tautologies of
  TST-4 are not fixed (one, `hr-clinic`, was already corrected in an earlier round), and this round's real
  contribution to it is negative evidence: the dashboard was reading green because nobody ran the suite in the
  posture CI uses.

DELETE` and runs *after* bootstrap — measured clause by clause, `timetable_periods` reads `arw` after migrate, `arw` after bootstrap, and **`arwd` after seed**. All 12 grants came back on every re-seed, including both Razorpay money ledgers, and bootstrap's baseline was scoped to schema `app` alone so the ledgers (in `plugin_razorpay`) were never covered even in principle. `server/src/grant-tightening.js` now revokes table-level DELETE across all 25 data schemas and re-grants it on the single table whose code path needs it, running LAST in both `bootstrap.js` and `seed.js`. Two late P0s were found *while* closing Phase 0 rather than by auditing for them — **SEC-22** (the settings validator was reading a stale stored manifest whose patterns could never match, so the validation it appeared to provide did not exist) and **DAT-6**'s true scope (2 doors + 1 branch, not the 14 the row claimed) — which is the honest reason to treat this phase as finished rather than proven finished: **both were found by *doing* the work, and a phase whose last two defects were discovered by implementation may have a third that only the next phase will surface.** |

### Phase ↔ register map — which phases the open rows actually belong to

**Why this table exists.** §6 numbers 34 items and §7 groups them into phases, but the register in §5 is organised by *discipline* (security, RBAC, data, API…), so nothing in the document said which phase a given open row belonged to. That gap had a cost this session: I reported a completion posture from the register (49 closed / 96 open) that was accurate and still misleading, because the phases are where the customer-visible work lives. A row-by-row count cannot tell you that the keystone is the least complete area.

**How to read it.** The phase items in §6 are grouped by phase in order — 6.1 = items 1–8 (Phase 0), 6.2 = items 9–14 (Phase 2), 6.3 = items 15–20 (Phase 3), 6.4 = items 21–28 (Phases 4–6), 6.5 = items 29–34 (Phases 1 and 6). The section→phase mapping below is derived from that structure plus each row's own text, and it is **inference, not a claim by the author**: where a row's phase is ambiguous the §6 item number is the authority, not this table.

| §5 section | Phase(s) | Rows | Open | Open rows | Why that phase |

**The three things this makes visible, neither of which is legible from the severity column:**

1. **Phase 2 (the keystone, §3) is the least complete phase and is the one everything else waits on.** RBAC, plugin masks and the org model all sit there, and §7's own rationale says doing the RBAC work before the org model means doing it twice. Any plan that closes P0/P1 rows in ID order will do that work in the wrong order — which is exactly the mistake this table exists to prevent.
2. **§5.5 (14 open of 15) is almost entirely Phase 6, not Phase 0.** It reads as the second-worst section by count and it is neither a trust blocker nor a customer-visible outage: integrators cannot write a retry or a page loop, and no school is harmed until someone tries to integrate. Severity-ordering it against §5.1 would be a category error.
3. **§5.4 carries rows in four different phases**, which is why "DAT-" prefixed findings keep turning out to be different classes of work — a trust blocker (DAT-6), the keystone (DAT-1/2's org model), a delivery bug (DAT-21's relay) and operational hygiene (DAT-9's indexes) all share one prefix.

### §8 — Phase 2 item 14: the client now ASKS the server what it may render · **PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-15**

**The fix.** New pure module `server/src/effective-access.js` (`SURFACE_DOOR` surface→door map, `reachableDoors()`
computed from the SAME `registeredRoutes`/`doorRoles` the gates use plus the caller's custom mask, `capabilitiesFor()`),
and a new **`GET /me/effective`** returning `role`, `role_key`, `custom`, `scope.classes` (a teacher's own
form-classes), `capabilities`, `nav` and the full reachable `doors`. It is a RENDER hint: every door still
re-decides per request. The client reads it via new `app/src/lib/effective.ts` (`canSurface`) + `apiMeEffective()`,
carried on the session by `AuthGate`; the **sidebar**, the **command palette** (which no longer shows the 21 staff
destinations to parent/student accounts, and points their AI item at their own `/parent/ask`/`/student/ask`) and
the **route Guard** (a staff deep-link the server refuses now renders an honest screen) all consult it.

**Evidence.** `tests/me-effective.test.js` **7/7** — including a DRIFT guard asserting every `SURFACE_DOOR` target
is a real registered door (unioned over five roles), and a mask moving a surface both ways (ALLOW adds `/library`
to a teacher-based role, DENY removes `/people`). `tests/frontend-grants.test.js` extended to **13/13**.
Mutation-probed: dropping the nav filter fails the source gate; making `reachableDoors` grant everything fails the
teacher test. Regression 52/52 + 66/66 + 40/40 across role, security, RLS and route-sensitive suites; `tsc -b`
clean; `oxlint` 0 errors; `vite build` passes; `permission-matrix` OK (267 routes).

**Honest scope.** The item is NOT fully closed: the Guard still carries the 43-entry `staffOnly` list for light
roles, and the effective check only fires for surfaces named in `SURFACE_DOOR` (unmapped surfaces remain on the
old rule). Replacing that list with a complete surface catalog — and moving palette/search off the demo-seed
brain — is the remaining half and is deliberately left open rather than implied done. The app has no DOM runner,
so client behaviour is asserted by pure-logic tests plus source checks, not a rendered browser test (TST-6).


### §8 — Session 2026-09-15 (L3-B): deployment honesty, docs honesty, and a PLG-4 regression the full run caught

**This session closed four register rows, advanced three, and fixed one regression — and the regression is the
entry worth reading, because it is the argument for the full-suite gate.**

- **OPS-18 (P1 → CLOSED) — the image can read its own code.** Every runtime source `COPY` in `server/Dockerfile`
  is now `COPY --chown=node:node`. **The fix is ownership, not `chmod`, and that is load-bearing:** git records
  only the executable bit, so a 600 → 644 normalisation in the repo would not survive the next clone and the
  defect would return on any strict-umask machine. Pinned by new `server/tests/ops-dockerfile.test.js` (3/3),
  which also asserts the non-root `USER` and that no second unguarded `COPY src` shadows the fix. Honest limit:
  static only — it does not prove a container boots (OPS-14).

- **OPS-19 (P2 → CLOSED) — and the row was STALE.** The `retention` healthcheck was already disabled in compose;
  the register carried it as `**Fixed this round**` while still labelled OPEN. Re-verified against the file and
  pinned so it cannot regress.

- **OPS-13 (P2 → grace half CLOSED, `/ready` half OPEN).** All five long-lived services carry
  `stop_grace_period: 30s`, so the declared 15s drain finishes before SIGKILL; asserted in `ops-deploy.test.js`.
  The `/ready`-keeps-answering-while-draining half is deliberately **not** claimed — it needs a live-server test
  this lane could not run mid-suite, so the row says so instead of implying done.

- **Item 29 / TST-3 red half — the number generator now has a gate, and the generator had a bug.** The first
  version printed *"Green status not determined"* over a log literally reading `fail 0`; green is now a property
  of the COUNTS, not of whether this invocation ran the suite. `docs/VERIFICATION.md` was stale at 220 tests
  (  from `server/test-run.log`) and is regenerated to the real **684 pass / 0 fail** from the final r52 log
  (`.coop/logs/ci-posture-r52.log`; it had earlier been corrected to r46's 663). NEW
  `server/tests/verification-artifact.test.js` (3/3) drives the real CLI against crafted green/red/no-tally logs,
  asserting the tool's three stated rules (a red run still writes the artifact; a missing tally is UNMEASURED,
  never 0).

- **Item 34 — customer-visible doc drift neutralised.** Corrected: `RELEASE-CERT.md` (a `ad6700a`-dated artifact
  now bannered HISTORICAL; the stale "114/114" already gone; "token revocation on logout" removed from the
  known-limits list because it shipped in 038), `RELEASE-NOTES.md`, `FEATURES-STATUS.md`, `DATA-FLOW.md`
  (**12 → 13 roles at four sites**, "200 routes" → generated), `SECURITY-MATRIX.md`, `ROLE-CAPABILITIES.md`
  (per-role door counts now say *re-derive from the matrix*; the measured census is recorded),
  `BACKEND-MAP.md` and `PROJECT-MAP.md` (snapshot counts → generated authority), and `app/README.md` (the false
  "no backend connected / 10 roles / 61-vs-56 screens" headline — the app runs against the live API and the
  kernel defines 13 roles).

- **TST-6/FE-9 and TST-14 (config halves).** CI's web job now runs `npm run lint` (oxlint, verified exit 0 —
  warnings reported, not gating); all CI jobs moved from **Node 22 to Node 26** to match the shipped runtime;
  `npm test` now runs the exact CI posture (`NODE_ENV=test OTP_DEV_ECHO=`, 120s timeout) so a local green
  predicts a CI green — the posture mismatch behind TST-8; and the app now has a Vitest/jsdom runner in CI.
  The cross-tenant sweep and broad route-render coverage remain open (TST-15/FE-9 follow-ups).

- **REGRESSION CAUGHT BY THE FULL RUN, and fixed (D-44 cross-zone repair on L3-A's `routes.js`).** r51's one
  failure was `tests/wave1.test.js:214` — *"catalog manifest preserved through enable"*. Root cause: PLG-4's
  enable door wrote the **whole on-disk manifest**, but six plugins (`ops.timetable`, `payments.razorpay`,
  `payments.upi`, `comms.whatsapp`, `accounting.tally`, `transport.gps`) declare their settings schema under the
  SNAKE_CASE key `settings_schema` while the stored row and the settings-validation door read CAMEL_CASE
  `settingsSchema` — so a re-enable silently dropped the schema for all six, making their settings
  unconfigurable. The codebase already documented this exact trap at `plugin-catalog.js:113`. The fix mirrors
  that module's reconciliation: keep the stored manifest as the base, overlay the file manifest, and normalise
  whichever schema key the file declared into the key the door reads. Verified: `wave1` 6/6,
  `plg-enable-metadata`+`plg-permissions`+`settings-contract` 13/13, `plugins`+`plugin-loader`+`plg-entitlement`+
  `rbac-plugin-edge` 17/17, and the fixed tree then passed the whole suite. **Lesson, and the reason this entry
  leads with it: the regression was invisible to every targeted suite and to the PR that introduced it; only the
  whole-suite run found it. A "targeted tests are green" claim is not a green suite.**

**The final green artifact.** `.coop/logs/ci-posture-r52.log` — **684 tests · 684 pass · 0 fail · 0 cancelled ·
0 skipped** (131 suites, 95 files, `NODE_ENV=test OTP_DEV_ECHO=`, ~54 min). This is the whole tree including the
PLG-4 regression fix, OPS-18/19/13, item 14, and both new test files. `docs/VERIFICATION.md` cites it.

*Honest scope: OPS-13's `/ready` work, the frontend runner, and the cross-tenant sweep are recorded at their
actual scope. No P0 was open in this session and none was closed; the work is P1/P2 honesty and the
definition-of-100's "docs contain no claim the code does not support" condition.*

### Working-tree follow-up — SEC-23

SEC-23 is implemented in the current tree: secret-class plugin settings are AES-256-GCM encrypted before
persistence with a tenant-bound derived key, server credential consumers decrypt them inside the process, and
registry/settings responses mask secret values. Legacy plaintext settings remain readable for migration
compatibility and are encrypted on the next settings save. The write-only settings form omits untouched masked
fields so a placeholder can never overwrite a credential. Coverage: `server/tests/settings-crypto.test.js`.

### Working-tree follow-up — OPS-16

Responses now expose Fastify's per-request identifier in `x-request-id`; error
responses also include the same value as `requestId` for client/log correlation.
Request IDs are correlated in responses and errors; durable request-log storage/reconciliation remains outside
this fix's scope.

The file-byte portion is now implemented: `scripts/backup.sh` writes and verifies
`files.tar.gz` beside each database dump, using `FILES_DIR` (or `server/storage`),
and the restore runbook extracts that archive beside the restored application.
Migration `094_erasure_replay_token.sql` adds an encrypted, tenant-bound subject
token and `scripts/replay-erasure-journal.js` replays all live erasure surfaces
inside an owner transaction before restored traffic is enabled. The token is
never logged or returned; `replayed_at` makes the operation idempotent.

### Working-tree follow-up — FE-3 and TST-6

FE-3 is implemented: `ItAdmin` reads the owner/IT-admin-only `/metrics` endpoint
and displays real process uptime, request/error counters, pool waiters, and
migration count; unsupported p95/auth-failure metrics remain unavailable rather
than fabricated. The frontend now has a Vitest/jsdom runner, setup, an initial
effective-access test, and a CI test step. Build and the initial 2-test suite
pass.

### Working-tree follow-up — DAT-12

Migration `092_integrity_followups.sql` adds fail-closed lifecycle checks for
library loan status, attendance/case actors, terminal action decisions, and
new lifecycle timestamps. They are `NOT VALID` to preserve historical rows for
an explicit cleanup/rebaseline.

### Working-tree follow-up — DAT-9 residual FK indexes

Migration `093_remaining_fk_indexes.sql` adds tenant-leading access paths for
attendance and guardian student joins, payment obligations, work-order asset /
assignee / reporter joins, library books, teacher submissions, PTM bookings,
and intervention plans. These are additive and preserve the earlier indexes
whose ordering serves a different query.

### Working-tree follow-up — PLG-10 capability resolution

The kernel no longer selects OTP, password-reset, payment-gateway, WhatsApp,
transport, or capability-display providers by hardcoded plugin-id queries.
Those providers declare capabilities in their manifests; the tenant-scoped
`enabledCapability()` resolver selects an enabled provider, and catalog
reconciliation persists the declarations for existing tenants. The transport
event budget is charged to the resolved provider, and the WhatsApp rail resolves
its template owner through `comms.whatsapp.templates`. Plugin-internal `ID`
constants remain intentionally local to the module that owns its doors.

### Working-tree follow-up — PLG-2c

`GET /plugins` now resolves each catalogue entry against the tenant's authored
plan matrix and returns `available` plus `requiredPlan`. The Plugin Manager
disables an unavailable Enable action and explains the upgrade path; disabling
an already-enabled plugin remains possible after a plan change.

### Working-tree follow-up — PLG-1b

The plugin loader now supplies a manifest-scoped kernel facade. Undeclared
`audit.request` and `events.emit` calls fail at the call site and create an
audit record; facilities without a kernel seam remain explicitly documented as
raw-SQL debt.

### Working-tree follow-up — PLG-2b

The enable door rejects incompatible kernel ranges and requires every
machine-readable dependency to be enabled at its exact declared version before
writing. Focused predicate coverage is in `server/tests/plg-enable-gates.test.js`.

### Working-tree follow-up — five small fixes

Migration `088_small_integrity_checks.sql` adds fail-closed checks for payment
methods, communication channels, transport-event kinds, and asset categories.
The checks are `NOT VALID` so legacy rows are preserved for explicit cleanup;
all new inserts and updates are constrained immediately. The parent assistant
also sanitizes its response boundary so its banned interpretive vocabulary is
enforced at runtime, not only by tests.

### Working-tree follow-up — five DAT-9 indexes

Migration `089_hot_tenant_indexes.sql` adds tenant-leading indexes for
communications, guardian phone lookup, user notifications, work-order events,
and route stops. The migration is additive and replay-safe.

### Working-tree follow-up — five more DAT-9 indexes

Migration `090_more_tenant_indexes.sql` adds tenant-leading indexes for
transport events, pending outbox rows, queued webhook deliveries, memory
entries, and student payments. The outbox and webhook indexes are partial and
match their workers' active-row predicates.

### Working-tree follow-up — OPS-20 and payment request validation

The dunning and reconciliation workers now use the shared explicit maintenance
database path instead of creating raw owner clients. Reconciliation emits through
the shared event spine with valid `warn` severity and correlation metadata. The
payment-initiation door also rejects unsupported methods before database work,
and live API errors preserve the server's `x-request-id`.

SEC-23 legacy plaintext settings now converge to encrypted envelopes during the
existing tenant plugin-catalog reconciliation; existing encrypted values are
left untouched.

### §8 — Session 2026-09-16 (next-10 hardening batch): verified, behaviourally pinned, and three regressions it left

The 16:06 batch (migrations 097–100 + the `next10-hardening` source gate) closed a set of P2 register rows: durable per-account login lockout (SEC-10 / 097), instance `bodyLimit` (SEC-14), dotenv-aware secret scanning (SEC-15), one kernel-owned multipart + raw-JSON parser (SEC-18), payment and case `Idempotency-Key` (API-9 / 098, 100), a 300-row register cap (API-10), JSON not-found + stable error codes (API-6), pagination `hasMore`/`OFFSET` (API-8), a refund ledger-inconsistency guard (DAT-13), a per-request duration histogram (OPS-9), and dispatcher claim leases (099).

The batch's own test (`next10-hardening.test.js`) is a SOURCE grep — it cannot tell a working fix from a deleted route. This session replaced it with behaviour: `next10-behavior.test.js` (10 tests) exercises the lockout (5th failure → 429, a valid password still refused while locked, expiry resets, success clears), payment/case idempotency (replay returns the original row, exactly one row, malformed key 400), the 64KiB event envelope bound, dispatcher lease semantics (a live claim is not stolen; a stale one is reclaimed and released on delivery; two concurrent ticks deliver each row once), the 300-row cap, honest pagination, and the refund ledger guard. `next10-followups.test.js` (6 tests) covers the earlier follow-ups: PLG-1b scoped-kernel refusal+audit, PLG-2c plan availability + 402, OPS-20 worker importability + spine emission, PLG-2b dependency-version reality, DAT-12 `NOT VALID` enforcement on new writes, and the DAT-9 indexes existing and valid. `secret-scan.test.js` (4 tests) proves the scanner fires on an unquoted `.env` secret and stays quiet on the repo's allow-listed dev values.

**Three regressions were found by running the whole suite, not by any targeted file:**

1. **The route registry was installed too late** (`src/routes.js`) — the `onRoute` hook was added ~120 lines into `registerRoutes`, so `GET /me` never entered `registeredRoutes` and the `SURFACE_DOOR` drift guard failed. The hook now installs before the first route, and the drift guard validates against the static route scanner (so a disabled plugin's door is not mistaken for a typo).
2. **Import-time config capture poisoned test files** (`scripts/health-probe.js`, `tests/build-info.test.js`, and the live half of `tests/ops-deploy.test.js`): importing a module that read `DATABASE_URL` before `setupTestDb()` bound the process to the ambient dev database, so the live suites authenticated against the wrong DB (`relation "auth.login_throttles" does not exist`). `health-probe` now loads `config` lazily inside its CLI branch, and `build-info` imports `app.js` after `setupTestDb`.
3. **`/metrics`' duplicate-series fix over-corrected** (`src/app.js`) — removing the generic `app.ops_counters` export restored a clean scrape but silently dropped every worker heartbeat (`webhook_last_tick_epoch`, workflow counters). The export is restored with a reserved-name exclusion so no series appears twice and no worker signal disappears.

**Five schema/test interactions the batch introduced and this session resolved:** migration 092's `attendance_marked_by_check` (correctly) rejects `marked_by IS NULL`, which stale fixtures in `exams`, `wave1` and `dat1` relied on; migration 096/097's new auth tables left `user_id` unconstrained, failing the DAT-2 census — closed by **migration 101** (a composite tenant-aligned FK on `mfa_challenges`, a single-column FK on `login_throttles`, both `NOT VALID`); `pack.cbse`'s stale dependency version made the pack unenablable (now `exams.assessment@1.0.1`, pinned against drift); the PLG-10 capability gate (`auth.password_reset` resolved from the stored manifest) required the comms-honesty fixture to persist the real rail manifest rather than a hand-written row; and the transport event door's enabled-provider precondition (`transport.gps`) required `api`, `security` and `governance` fixtures to switch the module on. A leaked `pg.Client` in `tests/exams.test.js` (no `try/finally`) also wedged the runner for 670s and marked every later file failed; that helper is fixed.

**The whole-suite run found 28 more failures, and they were the reason to run it rather than the targeted files.** They split into one REAL product bug and a set of fixtures the batch's contract changes had outgrown:

- **PRODUCT BUG — `/me/mfa/disable` 500'd on every call.** The state-machine rework moved the factor removal into `withUser` (the constrained `schoolos_app` role), which correctly has **no DELETE on `auth.mfa_secrets`/`auth.mfa_recovery_codes`** (D-35 / 057 make the vault kernel-only). The door now removes the factor, the recovery codes and the enrollment stamp in one `tx` (owner) transaction with the audit row — the security property is preserved and the door works. Pinned by `tests/l2-mfa.test.js` (TOTP codes now advance a step, because 095's single-use moving-factor guard correctly rejects replaying the enrollment code for the login challenge).
- **Capability-gate fixtures (PLG-10).** The OTP/reset/dunning doors now resolve providers through `manifest->'capabilities'`; hand-written plugin rows with no stored manifest were answered 503. Fixed in `comms-honesty`, `l2-rails-email-reset`, `rails-catalog-otp` and `fees-dunning` by persisting the REAL on-disk manifest — which also closed a **time-of-day flake**: the dunning quiet-hours test's manifest-less row fell back to the default 21:00–08:00 window, so it failed at night and passed by day.
- **Other stale expectations repaired, each against the code rather than by weakening it:** transport-door tests must enable `transport.gps` first (PLG-10); `pack.cbse` must have `exams.assessment` enabled first (PLG-2b); `comms.whatsapp` must be enabled for the WhatsApp template capability (`rails`); `/students` is now itself a scoped API-key door (`l2-api-keys-webhooks`); cookie sessions are `SameSite=Strict` (`l2-cookie-csrf`); `ops.visitors` arrives enabled via catalog `DEFAULT_ON` (`visitors`); a production config probe needs `PLUGIN_SETTINGS_KEY` (SEC-23); a small tally assertion had a `??`/`===` precedence bug.

**Final green artifact.** `.coop/logs/ci-posture-r55.log` — **717 tests · 717 pass · 0 fail · 0 cancelled · 0 skipped** (144 suites, 105 files, full CI posture). `docs/VERIFICATION.md` is regenerated from it (`--no-run --log`), and `node scripts/check.js`, `permission-matrix` (267 routes) and `scripts/secret-scan.mjs` are all green locally. The dev database's migration ledger was brought current (088–101) so the factory gate is meaningful locally too.

*Honest scope: this entry records verification and repairs, not new register closures. RBAC-1c, OPS-5b and RBAC-1b remain the open P2 rows.*

## 9. Fresh backend re-audit — 2026-09-17 (new findings, the pipeline law, and both scores)

**What this is.** A second, independent, code-level audit of the whole backend (and the client), run after the §8 work, because several §5 rows were called CLOSED while the *deployed pipeline* still disagreed. Six read-only lanes: auth/session, kernel routes (authorization/tenancy), the plugin tree, the data layer and migrations, workers/ops/CI, and frontend + test coverage. The lead verified every P0/P1 below by reading the code at the cited line. **These are additive findings, not a rewrite of §5** — the register rows stand as written; the IDs here (`AUTH-*`, `RTE-*`, `PLGX-*`, `DATX-*`, `OPSX-*`, `FEX-*`) are new.

### 9.1 The pipeline verification law — why a green marker can still be a lie

Two of the highest-severity new findings are **regressions of rows this document calls CLOSED**, and both fail the same way: a per-migration hardening is undone by a later stage of the *same* boot pipeline (`migrate → bootstrap → seed`), and a test hides it by re-applying the migration in-process.

- **DATX-1** — `scripts/bootstrap.js:39-40` runs `REVOKE ALL ... ; GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA app`, which **re-creates the table-level UPDATE/SELECT that migration 077 narrowed to column scope**. `seed.js` re-applies 074 but not 077; `grant-tightening.js` revokes DELETE only. Verified live: `has_table_privilege('schoolos_app','app.users','UPDATE') = true`, `webhook_subscriptions.sign_secret` readable, `enrollment_history`/`files` UPDATE restored. `tests/acl-column-scope.test.js` masks it by calling `applyRepair()` (re-running 077) inside each test. **SEC-2b/077 is not closed in any environment booted through the shipped pipeline.**
- **OPSX-1** — `docker-compose.yml:205` sets `NODE_ENV: development` on `comms-dispatcher`, so `sandboxAllowed(env)` is true and the dispatcher records a `logger` carrier as `status='sent'`. The only shipped stack therefore commits the exact PRD-13 inversion the module exists to prevent, and the only alert that would catch it (`comms_delivered_to_nobody`, `unless_env: development`) is **skipped by the committed cron** because `health-probe.js` defaults `env` to `development` (OPSX-6). **PRD-13 is not closed in the shipped stack.**

**The law:** *a hardening claimed CLOSED must be asserted by running the real pipeline (`migrate → bootstrap → seed`, and `compose up`), not by re-invoking the migration inside a test.* Any migration that tightens grants or policies must be routed through the shared, last-run module (`grant-tightening.js` / seed re-apply), or a later stage will silently re-open it. New CI gate required: after the full pipeline, assert the *tightened* state (e.g. `has_table_privilege(...,'UPDATE') = false` where 077 narrowed it, and no `NODE_ENV=development` on any worker).

### 9.2 New findings (severity · where · defect · fix)

**Auth & session (`AUTH-*`)**

| ID | Sev | Where | Defect | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUTH-1 | **P0 — CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `server/src/app.js:742-756` | `/me/switch` verifies the password against the **caller's own row**, then issues a session for the **target row found by unverified email**. Any account sharing a victim's email (self-signup `onboarding.js:52` or owner-provisioned arbitrary email) can switch into the victim's account in another tenant, including `owner`. The passing test masks it by forcing both rows to one password. | Verify `currentPassword` against the **target** row's `password_hash`; require the target's second factor. **CLOSED:** the target SELECT now carries `password_hash` + `mfa_enabled` and the door verifies the caller's input against the **target** hash (the caller's own password no longer opens another tenant's row). An MFA-enrolled target gets the standard JSON 401 challenge instead of a session. The test was the masking defect: `l2-multi-tenant.test.js` now gives the two same-email rows **different** passwords and asserts school A's password does NOT open school B; mutation-probed (weaken the target check → the test fails `200 !== 403`). |
| AUTH-2 | P1 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:541-543, 635, 667` | The OTP-verify, Google-callback and dev-SSO SELECTs omit `force_change`; `!!undefined === false`, so a temp-password account gets an **unrestricted** session via OTP/SSO, breaking D-37(c) "one shared issue path". | Add `force_change` to those three SELECTs. **CLOSED:** all three SELECTs now return `force_change`, so the shared `issueSession` stamps `mustChange`; pinned by `tests/auth-factor-seam.test.js` (OTP + dev-SSO token carries the claim and a staff door refuses it). |
| AUTH-3 | P1 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:310 vs 533-590, 612-671` | MFA challenge is issued only on the password branch; OTP/SSO never read `mfa_enabled`, so a staff TOTP factor is bypassed by phone or Google alone. | Run the same challenge branch after resolving the user on OTP/SSO. **CLOSED:** one shared `mfaChallenge()` helper is called on password, phone-OTP, Google and dev-SSO paths; OTP/Google/dev-SSO SELECTs carry `mfa_enabled`. `tests/auth-factor-seam.test.js` proves an MFA-enrolled account gets a 401 challenge (not a session) by OTP and SSO, and that the offered challenge completes to a session with the real TOTP. |
| AUTH-4 | P2 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17 (migration 103)** | `app.js:263-294` | Known email locks → 429; unknown → 401: an account-enumeration oracle and a 15-min DoS on any staff account for 5 unauthenticated requests. | Uniform response for unknown/known; key the counter without confirming existence. **CLOSED:** new `auth.request_throttles` (hashed identifier, no PII, kernel-only) is checked **before** the lookup and bumped on every failure for known and unknown alike, so both answer the same 429; a success clears it. `auth-throttle.test.js` pins the unknown-email lock; `next10-behavior.test.js` updated for the second lock. |
| AUTH-5 | P2 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:1111-1123`; `scripts/webhook-worker.js:91` | Webhook registration validates scheme/loopback only; `https://169.254.169.254/…`, `10.x`, `[fd00::1]` pass and the worker POSTs to them (**SSRF** from owner/itadmin). | Resolve host and reject private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT; re-check after DNS. **CLOSED:** new `src/plugins/_shared/webhook-target.js` resolves the host and refuses private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT/multicast ranges; used at registration **and** at delivery time. Loopback http remains the sandbox affordance. Pinned in `l2-api-keys-webhooks.test.js`. |
| AUTH-6 | P2 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:37,60` | `TRUST_PROXY=true` keys on the **leftmost** XFF hop (attacker-controlled when the proxy appends). | Use the rightmost untrusted hop / trusted hop count. **CLOSED:** the limiter uses the **rightmost** XFF hop (what the trusted proxy appended). The test's unique-XFF exercisability is preserved. Residual: multi-proxy / non-appending setups should key on a trusted hop count. |
| AUTH-7 | P2 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:924-948` | `POST /me/mfa/enroll` mints a secret from the session alone; a stolen session can bind an attacker factor and lock the victim out. | Require the password (as disable does). **CLOSED:** enroll now re-authenticates with the account password before minting/revealing a secret; `l2-mfa.test.js` updated to pass it. |
| AUTH-8 | P2 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17 (migration 103)** | `app.js:397-531` | No per-phone/account throttle on OTP request → SMS toll fraud/bombing (the residual SEC-10 named). | Durable per-phone cooldown + daily cap. **CLOSED:** `auth.request_throttles` enforces a per-phone 10/24h cap (always) plus a 60s cooldown on the real production send path; checked before the account lookup so it is uniform for registered/unregistered numbers. `auth-throttle.test.js` pins the cap. |
| AUTH-9..15 | P3 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `app.js:756, 87-96, 1049-1052, 298; 096/retention` | switch does not revoke the replaced jti; malformed cookie 500s; recovery codes 40-bit unsalted; no cumulative MFA-failure lockout; pre-auth membership/role disclosure; no `mfa_challenges` retention sweep; reset-request timing oracle. | Denylist on switch; try/catch decode; ≥10 bytes + keyed hash; per-account MFA lockout; defer picker past credential; add retention. **CLOSED:** switch denylists the replaced jti; `readCookie` catches URIError; recovery codes are 80-bit and HMAC-keyed; a cumulative per-account MFA lockout (10/15m) sits on `request_throttles`; the login picker verifies the password before listing schools; retention sweeps `mfa_challenges` + `request_throttles`; the reset door runs a decoy token INSERT on the unknown path. **Residual stated:** the reset email-send latency is not equalisable, and the OTP multi-tenant picker still reveals membership to a non-holder of the phone (documented). |
| RTE-15..20 | P3 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `routes.js:1539, 601-644, 2097, 1159, 1062; onboarding.js:70-166; core-lane-actions.js:113-139` | Refund audit overclaims reversal when there was no obligation; attendance audits no-ops/duplicates and re-queues notices; disable drains before the 404 check; unvalidated bigint/int/grade; unlocked JSONB config RMW; core-lane audit trusts a caller `tenantId`. | Branch the audit; dedupe/stable key; check row first; integer validators; lock/JSONB update; derive tenant from GUC. **CLOSED:** refund audit names "no linked obligation" when there is none; attendance notifies only NEWLY absent and drops the duplicate summary audit; plugin-disable checks existence before draining; `/transport/events` validates `delayMins`; the tenant-profile JSONB RMW takes `FOR UPDATE`; core-lane actions reject a `tenantId` that disagrees with the session GUC. |

The code comment claims atomic rollback. | `throw` (rolls back) instead of `return bad`, or validate before the UPDATE. **CLOSED:** both post-UPDATE refusals now `throw` (an object with `statusCode` for the >500 case), so `withUser` rolls back. `notice-execution.test.js` pins a 501-recipient notice → 413 **and** the action stays `open`; mutation-probed (disable the guard → the test fails). |

| ID | Sev | Where | Defect | Fix |

| OPSX-1 | P1 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `docker-compose.yml:205` | `NODE_ENV: development` on `comms-dispatcher` disables sandbox honesty → log-only sends recorded `sent` in the shipped stack. | `${NODE_ENV:-production}`. **CLOSED:** the service now runs `${NODE_ENV:-production}` + `WORKER_ROLE: "1"` (the supported way to skip the HTTP-surface prod guards for a non-HTTP process, already used by the outbox/retention/webhook/workflow workers), so `sandboxAllowed(env)` is false and a `logger` carrier can no longer be recorded as `sent`. `tests/ops-deploy.test.js` remains green. |
| OPSX-2 | P1 — **CLOSED 2026-09-17** | `docker-compose.yml:58-91,247`; `files.store:68`; `backup.sh:79` | `api` has no files volume (`storage` lives in the container) while backup reads the **host** `server/storage` → every recreate destroys documents and `files.tar.gz` is empty while the test certifies it. | Named `files` volume at `FILES_DIR`; set it in compose and backup. **CLOSED:** `api` sets `FILES_DIR: /data/files` and mounts the named `files` volume; a `files-backup` one-shot (profile `ops`) archives the volume to `./backups/files/files-<date>.tar.gz` (verified with `tar -tzf`), and `backup.sh` warns when a host tree is absent so the gap is visible. Pinned by `ops-deploy.test.js`. |

**New findings / notes from the campaign (not previously in the register):**

1. **The migration-runner transaction leak (DATX-6) was real and silent.** Eight applied migrations carried a
   top-level `BEGIN;…COMMIT;`; because editing an applied file is checksum-frozen, the fix lives in the
   runner (`stripTopLevelTransactions`) and is pinned. Any future migration with a top-level wrapper is now
   neutralised rather than partially committing.
2. **Two locks now guard login** (`auth.login_throttles` per-account + `auth.request_throttles` per-hashed-
   email). They are consistent, but a future edit must keep both — recorded so it is not "simplified" away.
3. **AUTH-9 changed a client-visible contract:** `/me/switch` now retires the replaced session. `tenantSwitch`
   / `Shell` must treat the pre-switch bearer as dead (it already replaces the token, so no code change is
   required, but the assumption "the old session still works" is gone).
4. **Residuals deliberately left, each stated rather than hidden:** reset email-send timing; the OTP
   multi-tenant picker's membership disclosure to a non-holder of the phone; the audit chain's
   `(created_at, id)` ordering (writer and verifier agree); `request_throttles` is global (no tenant) by
   design and has no FK, so it must be added to the DAT-2 census allowlist if the census is extended.
5. **Migrations 102–105** were added (RLS/pipeline repairs, request throttles, policy+constraint repairs,
   audit-verify boundary). The full `migrate → bootstrap → seed` pipeline is green through them.

### 9.6 Working-tree follow-up — Phase 0 re-opened and re-closed (2026-09-17)

The §9.5 conclusion was that Phase 0 is not complete until its rows are asserted **against the deployed pipeline**. This batch
closes the six Phase-0 rows the re-audit re-opened (`AUTH-1`, `AUTH-2`, `AUTH-3`, `DATX-1`, `DATX-2/3/4`, `OPSX-1`).

- **AUTH-1 (P0)** — `/me/switch` now verifies the caller's input against the **target** account's `password_hash`, and an
  MFA-enrolled target is challenged instead of issued a session. The old passing test was itself the masking defect: it
  gave the two same-email rows one password, so it could not tell the caller's factor from the target's. It now uses two
  different passwords and asserts school A's password does **not** open school B. Mutation-probed.
- **AUTH-2 / AUTH-3** — one shared `mfaChallenge()` and `force_change` on the OTP/Google/dev-SSO SELECTs. New
  `tests/auth-factor-seam.test.js` drives a real OTP and a real TOTP secret: a temp-password account's OTP/SSO session
  carries `mustChange` and is refused at a staff door, and an MFA-enrolled account gets a challenge (not a session) by
  phone and by SSO, which then completes with the authenticator code.
- **DATX-1 (P0)** — `tightenAppRoleColumns()` is the 077 column scope in the shared tightening module, applied as the
  last step of **both** `bootstrap.js` and `seed.js`. `tests/acl-column-scope.test.js` now begins with a test that reads
  the **post-pipeline** state with no `applyRepair()`, which is the assertion the register said was missing; removing the
  bootstrap/seed call fails it.
- **DATX-2/3/4** — migration **102** enables RLS on `teacher.submission_needs_linking`, makes `app.purge_staged_rows`
  fail closed on an unset GUC, and rebuilds the 083 policy through `app.current_tenant()`; the 074 invariant test
  ("NO policy inlines `current_setting`") is green.
- **OPSX-1** — `comms-dispatcher` runs `${NODE_ENV:-production}` + `WORKER_ROLE=1`, so the shipped stack cannot record a
  log-only carrier as a delivery.

**Verification:** targeted regression **44/44** (`acl-column-scope`, `l2-multi-tenant`, `auth-factor-seam`, `l2-mfa`,
`audit-coverage`, `rls-policy-hardening`, `dat2-reference-integrity`) plus **57/57** (`ops-deploy`, `test-hygiene`,
`l1-security-sprint`, `secret-scan`). A full CI-posture run is the remaining gate before these rows are called verified
end-to-end; the register rows above are marked CLOSED on the targeted evidence, and the pipeline assertion is the new
durable guard.

The same session then took the next **P1** batch: **RTE-1** (the `withUser` commit-on-`return` trap now `throw`s, so a
refused notice or a failed admissions executor rolls the status flip back), **RTE-2** (refund reads and checks the
obligation `FOR UPDATE` **before** the gateway call), **RTE-3** (`/admissions/followups` moved from `me` to the funnel's
staff gate), **PLGX-1** (the student noticeboard no longer serves the tenant-wide event feed), and **PLGX-2**
(`ops.timetable/autofill` applies the same `class_assignments` ownership rule and validates the date). Each
carries a behavioural pin; RTE-1 and RTE-2 were mutation-probed. A latent UTC-vs-IST flake in
`prd2-class-assignments.test.js` (it asserted the UTC date while the door defaults to the IST school day, DAT-7) was
fixed to `istToday()` while extending that file.

The **OPSX batch** then closed OPSX-2 (a named `files` volume + `files-backup`, so uploaded documents survive a
recreate), OPSX-3 (fleet-wide `schoolos_fleet_*` queue gauges with a second-tenant test), OPSX-4 (`metrics.read` API
scope so a machine identity can scrape `/metrics`), OPSX-5 (the API heartbeat check reads only the API-owned rows,
so a fresh backup no longer masks a dead API), OPSX-6 (`NODE_ENV=production` on the probe cron line), OPSX-7
(`tickOnce` commits outbox discovery before webhook delivery, so delivery no longer starves emission) and OPSX-8
(the rule→metric link is now data — every rule declares `metric:` and the gate iterates it). Verified 47/47 and
36/36 across `ops-deploy`, `rls-boundary`, `l2-api-keys-webhooks`, `outbox-discovery`.

The **AUTH hardening batch** then closed AUTH-5 (new `webhook-target.js` resolves the host and refuses
private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT at registration **and** delivery — SSRF), AUTH-6 (the rate limiter keys on the
**rightmost** XFF hop the trusted proxy appended, not the attacker-supplied leftmost) and AUTH-7 (`/me/mfa/enroll`
now requires the account password before it mints and reveals a TOTP secret). Verified 24/24 across
`l2-api-keys-webhooks`, `l2-mfa`, `onboard`. **AUTH-4 (account-enumeration login oracle / lockout DoS) and AUTH-8
(per-phone OTP throttle) remain OPEN**: both need a durable identifier-keyed throttle, which migration 101's
`login_throttles` FK (and the DAT-2 no-FK census) currently block — that is a schema change of its own, not a
one-line fix, and it is recorded here rather than half-done.

### 9.8 §9.2 register — next closure batch (2026-09-17, second session)

The baseline r57 full run had three failures, all in the §9.2 campaign's own tooling; each was fixed before this
batch and re-verified: `test-hygiene` was flagging `rls-boundary.test.js:150` as `no-assertion-in-test` because
the justification ledger sat inside the test body (moved OUT, per the file's own note); the owner-path pin was
stale at 110 while the code held 118 (corrected); and `mfa-recovery-race` was green after its fixture was fixed.
A full CI-posture run is the gate for the batch below.

**Rows CLOSED this batch (P2 unless noted):**

- **FEX-3 (P1) — session JWT out of `localStorage`.** The bearer is now in-memory only; a legacy stored token is
  cleared once, and the durable credential is the HttpOnly cookie (with `X-CSRF-Token` on mutations). Every
  hand-rolled transport across `liveApi`, `parentApi`, `coordApi`, `studentApi`, `teacherFixtures`, `useKernel`,
  `tenantSwitch`, `MfaSection`, `ChangePassword`, `dataset` and the visitor-verify/print/wellbeing/roles screens
  now routes through the single `apiRequest` (memory bearer + same-origin cookie + CSRF); the old
  `localStorage.getItem('schoolos_token')` reads are gone (grep = 0 outside the migration cleanup). Evidence:
  `app/src/test/` vitest green; `tsc -b` clean.
- **FEX-5 (P2) — surfaces map to real doors and fail closed.** `/group`, `/settings`, `/rails`,
  `/plugins/settings[/:id]` no longer proxy the `me` door `GET /plugins`; they map to owner/itadmin doors
  (`GET /metrics`, `POST /plugins/:id/settings`). `canSurface` now fails closed on a surface the server did not
  name. `me-effective.test.js` gains a coverage assertion (every `App.tsx` route is in `SURFACE_DOOR`) and
  teacher-vs-owner assertions for the four admin surfaces; `effective.test.ts` + `frontend-grants.test.js`
  updated (strengthened, not weakened). **22/22 green.**
- **DATX-11/12/13 (P2/P3) — migration 106.** Fifteen tenant tables gain a tenant-leading index; six
  prefix-covered redundant indexes are dropped (re-applied after the seed replay via
  `grant-tightening.js#dropRedundantIndexes`, because seed replays 032); `app.class_assignments` is granted in a
  migration rather than relying on bootstrap. Evidence: `tests/index-hygiene.test.js` 3/3 against the real
  migrated schema.
- **FEX-12 (P2) — the runner itself has behaviour tests.** `migrate.js` accepts `MIGRATIONS_DIR` (testable
  without touching the repo migrations) and `tests/migration-runner-behaviour.test.js` drives the real script:
  concurrent single-writer (advisory lock, applied exactly once), mid-file failure rolls back with no partial
  schema, edited/NULL-checksum drift refused, explicit `MIGRATION_REBASELINE` heals. 4/4.
- **FEX-14 (P2) — recipient fan-out/cap.** `fanOut` is exported and `tests/comms-fanout.test.js` pins
  normalise+dedupe, the 500 cap as a refusal (501 refused, nothing sent), partial-delivery honesty (2/3 →
  `ok:false`), and a real 300-guardian row whose receipt says `300/300`. 9/9 with `test-hygiene`.
- **FEX-15 (P2) — money races + `refunded_cents`.** Migration 107 adds `app.payments.refunded_cents` with
  `CHECK (refunded_cents <= amount_cents)` and backfills historic full refunds; the refund door records it.
  `payments-refund.test.js` gains concurrent-confirm, concurrent-refund and over-refund-CHECK cases. 10/10.
- **FEX-9 (P2) — `strict: true` + screen smoke.** `app/tsconfig.app.json` enables `strict` (`tsc -b` clean) and
  `app/src/test/screens-load.smoke.test.tsx` imports the whole screen tree (App first, as production does). It
  caught a real circular-evaluation TDZ (`Slot → useKernel → App → registerWidgets → Slot`); the widget map now
  lives in the import-free leaf `plugins/widgetRegistry.ts`. Vitest 5/5.
- **FEX-10/11 (P3) — fabricated AI/telemetry fields deleted.** `model`/`latencyMs`/`tokens`/`cost` removed from
  `AiTrace` and all ~16 object literals; IT-admin integration cards no longer show a measured-looking
  "connected"/"last use" — they render "Not configured" and a dash, since no integration-health door exists.

**Rows PARTIALLY closed:** FEX-6/7/8 — the hand-rolled transports were collapsed onto `apiRequest` and the
wellbeing allowlist now matches the server (teacher removed; copy promised it never admitted them), but the
"two role sources" (`live.role` vs the demo switch's `session.roleId`) and the client-only `ROLE_CAMPUS` remain,
and they are FEX-4's problem. FEX-13 — the backup test is still source-string based; a real
backup→restore→erasure-replay drill is not yet written.

**Still OPEN:** FEX-2 (23 screens on the mock plane; parity gate report-only), FEX-4 (~111 hand-coded client role
checks / role arrays), OPSX-10 (offsite/PITR/restore drill — host infra), OPSX-11/12 (parity `--enforce` and the
fresh-volume compose smoke are coupled to FEX-2; the CI `verification-artifact.js` generation + upload steps are
added, which is the part independent of FEX-2).

**New migrations:** 106 (index hygiene + grants), 107 (`refunded_cents`). Next free number: **108**.
**New tests:** `comms-fanout`, `migration-runner-behaviour`, `index-hygiene`,
`app/src/test/screens-load.smoke.test.tsx`.

### 9.9 §9.2 register — second closure batch (2026-09-17, evening)

Continuing the campaign to the ceiling. This batch closes the four non-frontend residual rows and the
frontend foundation:

- **FEX-13 (P2) — a real restore drill.** `tests/backup-restore-drill.test.js` does the whole loop against
  real tools: insert a guardian phone + a DPDP journal row, `pg_dump`, `pg_restore` into a fresh database,
  run the shipped `replay-erasure-journal.js`, and assert the phone is gone in the **restored** copy and the
  journal marked replayed. It also runs `backup.sh` end to end and verifies its artifacts. (The key had to be
  pinned for both processes — the test process does not load `.env`, the child does.)
- **OPSX-10 (P2) — off-host, encrypted, PITR, drill.** New `ops/backup/`: `encrypt-offsite.sh` (AES-256
  archive, verified decrypt, sha256), `restore-drill.sh` (restore → replay → measured RTO),
  `pitr.compose.yml` (`wal_level=replica` + `archive_mode=on` + `archive_command` to a non-pgdata volume),
  and a README with runbook + honest limits. `tests/ops-dr.test.js` executes the encryption and asserts a
  wrong key fails. Residual stated: production off-host location and the first live PITR activation are
  operational, not code.
- **FEX-2 (P1) — the mock plane is gone from `services/` and the parity gate ENFORCES.** The no-HTTP mock
  layer moved to `app/src/demo/seedServices.ts` (0 `services` importers); every live screen that uses it is
  labelled sample data. `parity-gate.js --enforce` now exits 0: 0 mock importers and 0 unresolved write
  doors, with 61 doors explicitly allowlisted as API-only/machine/plugin-runtime with a stated reason.
  `tests/parity-gate.test.js` makes enforcement part of `npm test`; CI runs `--enforce`.
- **OPSX-11/12 (P2) — CI builds and boots the artifact.** CI now runs `verification-artifact.js` (generate +
  upload) and a new `compose-smoke` job that builds the image on fresh volumes and asserts `/ready` + `/health`.
- **FEX-4 / FEX-6/7 (P1) — foundation landed, not complete.** New `app/src/lib/access.ts` gives one role
  notion (`useLiveRole` / `useLightRole` / `useRoleOrGrant` via the custom-mask matcher); the app chrome
  (`App.tsx` Frame, `Shell.tsx` Topbar) now derives light/staff from the **authenticated** role instead of the
  dual `session.roleId`/`live.role` sources. **Residual:** the ~111 hard-coded per-screen role arrays are not
  yet migrated to `useRoleOrGrant`; that is the remaining FEX-4 work.

New tests this batch: `backup-restore-drill`, `ops-dr`, `parity-gate`.
